a single oral dose of the FDC tablet following a high-fat meal increased VNZ AUC0­∞, AUC0­tlast,
and Cmax by approximately 6-fold compared to fasted state. A low-fat meal increased VNZ
AUC0­∞, AUC0­tlast, and Cmax by approximately 4-fold compared to fasted state. Food had no
large impact on TEZ exposures overall. Administration of a single oral dose of the FDC tablet
following a high-fat meal increased D-IVA AUC0­∞, AUC0­tlast, and Cmax by approximately 4fold, 4-fold, and 5-fold, respectively, compared to fasted state. A low-fat meal increased D-IVA
AUC0­∞, AUC0­tlast, and Cmax by approximately 3-fold, 3-fold, and 4-fold, respectively, compared
to fasted state.

Conclusions

drug was given as FDC tablets relative to when it was given as separate tablets as VNZ 10 mg
[Form A], TEZ 100 mg, and D-IVA 250 mg (Part A).
Administration of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA as FDC tablets under fed (high- and low-fat) conditions
resulted in increased exposure to VNZ (up to ~6-fold for AUC0­∞ and Cmax) and D-IVA (up to
~4-fold and ~5-fold for AUC0­∞ and Cmax, respectively) compared to fasted conditions. Exposure
to TEZ under fed conditions remained comparable to fasted conditions.
14.2.2.4. Study VX22-121-009 (Study 009)
Title
A Phase 1, Randomized, Open-label, Crossover Study to Evaluate the Relative Bioavailability of
a Fixed-dose Combination Tablet of VX-121 [VNZ], Tezacaftor, and Deutivacaftor in Healthy
Adult Subjects
164
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Study Period
September 15, 2022 to November 9, 2022
Objective
Primary Objective
To evaluate the relative BA of a test FDC tablet of VNZ [Form D] /tezacaftor
(TEZ)/deutivacaftor (D-IVA) compared to a reference FDC tablet of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
Secondary Objective
To assess the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA administered as FDC tablets
Study Design
This was a phase 1, randomized, open-label, 2-period, 2-sequence, crossover study to evaluate
the relative BA of a test FDC tablet of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA compared to a reference FDC tablet of
VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. The study design is presented in Figure 17.
In each dosing period, healthy subjects received a single dose of VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/ DIVA 250 mg orally, either as test FDC tablets (supplied as five tablets of VNZ [Form D]
4 mg/TEZ 20 mg/D-IVA 50 mg) or as reference FDC tablets (supplied as two tablets of VNZ
[Form D] 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg). The Washout Period between doses was 14 days.

14.2.3. Drug Interaction Studies
14.2.3.1. Study VX21-121-006 (Study 006)
Title
A Phase 1, Open-label Study to Evaluate the Pharmacokinetic Effects Between VX-121 [VNZ],
Tezacaftor, and Deutivacaftor in Healthy Subjects
Study Period
September 10, 2021 to December 7, 2021
Objective
Primary Objectives
•

To assess the effect of TEZ and D-IVA on the PK of VNZ

•

To assess the effect of VNZ and TEZ on the PK of D-IVA

•

To assess the effect of VNZ and D-IVA on the PK of TEZ

Secondary Objective
•

To assess the safety and tolerability of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA when administered separately
and together

Study Design
A phase 1, open-label, 2-cohort crossover study to evaluate the PK of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA
when administered separately and together (Figure 18). During Cohort 1, healthy adult subjects
received VNZ monotherapy in Dosing Period 1 and VNZ in combination with TEZ and D-IVA
in Dosing Period 2. During Cohort 2, healthy adult subjects received D-IVA monotherapy in
Dosing Period 1, TEZ monotherapy in Dosing Period 2, and VNZ in combination with TEZ and
D-IVA in Dosing Period 3. VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA (as monotherapy and in combination) were
administered orally in the fed state.

Results
Table 93 summarizes the PK metrics of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA after multiple dose
administration as monotherapy or in TC. Generally, exposures (Cmax,ss and AUC) of VNZ, TEZ,
and D-IVA increased when administered in TC compared to monotherapy. Time to maximum
concentration was similar between treatments for all analytes except VNZ, which reached
median tmax earlier (5 hours) following administration in TC as compared to monotherapy
(median tmax of 10 hours).
The exposures (Cmax,ss and AUC) of active major metabolites (M1-TEZ and M1-D-IVA) were
increased following administration of TEZ and D-IVA in TC (with VNZ) compared to
monotherapy (Table 94).

The PK interaction between VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA administered as monotherapy or TC was
assessed statistically on the log transformed Cmax,ss and AUC (AUClast and AUCtau at steady
state). The GLSM ratios and their associated 90% CIs were reported and are presented in Table
95 for VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA. VNZ Cmax,ss and AUC increased 48% and 54%, respectively,
when VNZ was administered in TC with TEZ/D-IVA compared to VNZ administered alone. The
90% CIs ranged from 1.28 to 1.72 for Cmax,ss and 1.34 to 1.77 for AUC.
The extents of exposure increase of TEZ and D-IVA administered in TC compared to
administered alone were less than that observed in VNZ. TEZ Cmax,ss and AUC increased 29%
and 35%, respectively, and M1-TEZ Cmax,ss and AUC were increased 17% and 11%,
respectively. D-IVA Cmax,ss and AUC increased 18% and 27%, respectively, M1-D-IVA Cmax,ss
and AUC increased 27% and 39%, respectively.

Conclusions
Following 14 days once daily (QD) regimen of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA as administered in TC,
exposures of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA and their respective metabolites were all greater compared to 14
days QD treatment of VNZ, TEZ and D-IVA when administered as monotherapy.
•

VNZ Cmax,ss and AUC increased 48% and 54%, respectively.

•

TEZ Cmax,ss and AUC increased 29% and 35%, respectively, and M1-TEZ Cmax,ss and AUC
were increased 17% and 11%, respectively.

•

D-IVA C max,ss and AUC increased 18% and 27%, respectively, M1-D-IVA Cmax,ss and AUC
increased 27% and 39%, respectively.

For additional assessment of these results and their potential impact on assessing TC efficacy and
safety, refer to Section 6.1.

Note: AUC0­∞ was excluded from inferential statistics when AUC extrapolated >30%
Abbreviations: AUC0­∞, area under the concentration-time curve estimated to infinity; AUC0-tlast, area under the concentration-time
curve from time 0 to the last measurable concentration; CI, confidence interval; Cmax, maximum plasma concentration;
GLSM, geometric least squares mean; PK, pharmacokinetic; VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor

However, the effect of itraconazole on exposure of VNZ is underestimated in this study as the
PK sampling scheme in Period 2 (up to 480 hours) hardly covered three half-lives of VNZ (185
hours ×3) (Figure 20). A physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model estimated an
increase of AUC0­inf of VNZ by 10.5-fold with sufficient time interval for evaluation. For details,
refer to Section 14.5.2.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Figure 20. Mean (+SD) Concentration-Time Profiles of VNZ With or Without Coadministered With
Itraconazole (Linear Scale)

Source: Figure 11-1 CSR Study VX21-121-007
Abbreviations: h, hour; SD, standard deviation; VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor

Conclusions
When coadministered with itraconazole, a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor, VNZ Cmax was increased by
1.55-fold, AUC0­tlast increased by 5.55-fold, and AUC0­∞ increased by 6.37-fold. The effect of
itraconazole on VNZ AUC values is an underestimate due to the limitation of PK sampling
scheme. The PBPK model estimates a 10.5-fold increase of AUC0­∞.

14.2.4.1. Study VX20-121-004 (Study 004)

Metabolism, and Excretion of 14C-VX-121 [VNZ] Following Single Oral Administration in
Healthy Subjects
Study Period
January 25, 2021 to February 27, 2021
Objective
Primary Objectives
•

To determine the routes and rate of elimination and recovery of total radioactivity (TRA)
after a single oral dose of 14C-VNZ

•

To characterize the PK of VNZ and TRA after a single oral dose of 14C-VNZ

Secondary Objectives
•

To profile and identify the metabolites of VNZ, if any, in the urine, plasma, and feces
following administration of a single oral dose of 14C-VNZ

•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of a single oral dose of 14C-VNZ

A phase 1, nonrandomized, open-label, mass balance study in healthy male subjects (Figure 22).

179
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Results
Absorption
The mean concentration-time profiles of unchanged VNZ in plasma and of TRA in plasma and
whole blood are presented in Figure 23. Following a single dose of 14C-VNZ 20 mg/200 μCi
administered as an oral solution, the unchanged VNZ in plasma and the TRA in plasma and
whole blood reached peak concentrations after approximately 8 hours. The mean t1/2 was similar
for TRA in plasma (68.9 hours) and whole blood (71.8 hours), which was slightly longer than the
mean t1/2 of unchanged VNZ in plasma (60.8 hours).
Figure 23. Mean (SD) Concentration-Time Profiles of Unchanged VNZ in Plasma and TRA in
Plasma and Whole Blood in Scales

Source: Figure 11-1 CSR Study VX20-121-004
Abbreviation: h, hour; SD, standard deviation; TRA, total radioactivity VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor

Distribution
The ratios of VNZ to TRA in plasma and the blood-to-plasma ratio for TRA are presented in
Table 103 for both AUC0­∞ (hereafter referred to as AUC) and Cmax. As determined by the AUC
ratio for VNZ to TRA in plasma, unchanged VNZ accounted for approximately 77% of the TRA
in plasma after a single dose of 14C-VNZ 20 mg/200 μCi administered as an oral solution. The
TRA in blood was approximately 56% of the TRA in plasma, suggesting that there was no
preferential distribution of VNZ into red blood cells.

180
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Table 103. AUC and Cmax Ratios for Unchanged VNZ to TRA in Plasma and Blood TRA to Plasma
TRA After Administration of a Single 20 mg/200 μCi Oral Dose of 14C-VNZ in Healthy Subjects

Source: Table 11-2 CSR Study VX20-121-004
Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; Cmax, maximum plasma concentration; CV, coefficient of variation;
N, number of subjects; TRA, total radioactivity; VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor

Metabolism
VNZ accounted for the majority of the total circulating radioactivity in plasma (mean =96.2%).
M19-VNZ accounted for 3.8% of the total circulating radioactivity, indicating that no major
metabolites were observed. VNZ was mainly cleared by metabolism in humans as the mean
unchanged parent recovered in excreta accounted for less than 10% of the administered
radioactive dose. The most abundant metabolite in feces was M6-VNZ, which accounted for
26.1% of the administered radioactive dose, followed by M55-VNZ, M19-VNZ, M57-VNZ, and
M9-VNZ, which accounted for 7.4%, 6.9%, 6.9%, and 6.8% of the administered dose,
respectively. Urine metabolites were not profiled because only approximately 0.5% of the
radioactive dose was recovered in urine.
Elimination
The TRA in urine and in feces was used for calculation of the Ae of the administered dose. The fe
represents the recovery of TRA. The mean cumulative percentage of TRA recovered in each
matrix with respect to time is presented in Figure 24.
The mass balance and overall recovery of TRA are summarized in Table 104. Mean recovery of
TRA over 408 hours (17 days) postdose was 0.502% in urine and 91.6% of the radioactive dose
recovered in feces, demonstrating that excretion in urine is minimum and elimination in feces
accounted for most of the excreted dose. VNZ was mainly cleared by metabolism in humans as
the mean unchanged parent recovered in excreta accounted for less than 10% of the administered
radioactive dose.

subjects; TRA, total radioactivity; VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor

Conclusions
VNZ was generally safe and well tolerated at the evaluated dose of 20 mg/200 μCi in healthy
subjects in this study. Unchanged VNZ accounted for most of the total circulating radioactivity
in plasma and there were no major circulating metabolites of VNZ. VNZ was mainly cleared by
metabolism in humans. VNZ was primarily excreted as metabolites in the feces. Renal excretion
of VNZ was negligible.

14.2.4.2. Study VX21-121-008 (Study 008)
Title
A Phase 1, Open‑label Study to Assess the Pharmacokinetics and Safety of a Single Dose of

Study Period
July 22, 2022 to March 16, 2023
Objective
Primary Objective
•

To evaluate the PK of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites when administered in
subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (MHI) compared to the PK in matched healthy
subjects

Secondary Objective
•

To assess the safety and tolerability of a single dose of VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA
125 mg FDC tablet in subjects with MHI and in matched healthy subjects

Study Design
This was a phase 1, nonrandomized, open-label study evaluating a single dose of orally
administered VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in subjects with MHI (Cohort 1) and matched healthy subjects
(Cohort 2). Subjects in Cohort 1 had to meet the criteria for Child-Pugh Class B: 7 to 9 to be
eligible for enrollment. The study design is shown in Figure 25.

than those in matched healthy subjects, with 90% CI ranges including 1. Total M1-D-IVA in
MHI subjects were approximately 30% lower than those in matched healthy subjects.
14.2.4.3. Study VX22-121-013 (Study 013)
Study VX22-121-013 is a thorough QT study which has been reviewed at QT-Interdisciplinary
Review Team. Up to 60 mg QD dose of VNZ [Form A] has been investigated in this study. For
details, refer to the QT study review dated July 16, 2024.

14.2.5. Dose-Ranging Studies
14.2.5.1. Study VX18-121-101 (Study 101)
Title
A Phase 2, Randomized, Double-blind, Controlled Study to Evaluate the Safety and Efficacy of
VX-121 [VNZ] Combination Therapy in Subjects Aged 18 Years and Older With Cystic Fibrosis

186
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Study Period
April 30, 2019 to December 10, 2019
Objective
Primary Objectives
•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ in TC with TEZ/D-IVA

•

To evaluate the efficacy of VNZ in TC with TEZ/D-IVA

Secondary Objectives
•

To evaluate the PD effect of VNZ in TC with TEZ/D-IVA

•

To evaluate the PK of VNZ when administered in TC with TEZ/D-IVA

•

To evaluate the PK of TEZ, D-IVA, and their respective metabolites when administered in
TC with VNZ

Study Design
Part 1
Subjects heterozygous for F508del and a minimal function variant [F/MF]. Subjects must remain
on a stable CF medication (and supplement) regimen (other than protocol-specified changes in
medications that affect CFTR processing or function) for their CF from 28 days before the Day 1
Visit (Parts 1) through the Safety Follow-up Visit.
Part 1 had a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group design that evaluated
VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA (Figure 26). Subjects were randomized in a 2:2:1:1 ratio to the TC-20 mg:TC10 mg:TC-5 mg:placebo arms.

TEZ and IVA: The doses of TEZ and IVA were the same as those evaluated in phase 3 studies of
TEZ/IVA (TEZ: 100 mg QD; IVA: 150 mg every 12 hours [q12h]).
D-IVA: A D-IVA dose of 150 mg QD was selected for evaluation based on PK, safety, and
efficacy results from prior studies of D-IVA administered as monotherapy or in TC with other
medications that affect CFTR processing or function. A 150-mg QD dose of D-IVA results in
similar exposures as IVA when administered alone at a dose of 150 mg q12h, including AUC0­24h
and Ctrough.

Conclusions
In the context of TC (i.e., in combination with 100 mg TEZ and 150 mg IVA), 10 mg VNZ (in
Form A) once daily dose demonstrated a higher ppFEV1 response (primary efficacy endpoint)
compared to 5 mg and 20 mg in CF subjects with F/MF genotype. Although a small numerical
dose-response trend was observed for SwCl and CFQ-R RD score (secondary endpoints) from
5 mg to 20 mg, the dose selection of carrying 10 mg VNZ (Form A, which translate into 20 mg
in Form D) into phase 3 trials is reasonable.
14.2.5.2. Study VX18-561-101 (Study 561-101)
Title
A Phase 2, Randomized, Double-blind Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of VX-561 [DIVA] in Subjects Aged 18 Years and Older With Cystic Fibrosis
Study Period
April 17, 2019 to August 20, 2020

197
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Objective
Primary Objective
•

To evaluate the efficacy of D-IVA

Secondary Objectives
•

To evaluate the PD effect of D-IVA

•

To evaluate the PK of D-IVA, IVA, and relevant metabolites

•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of D-IVA

Study Design
This was a phase 2, randomized (ratio of 1:2:2:2:1), double-blind, parallel-group, and activecontrolled study of D-IVA monotherapy (Figure 34). The study evaluated four dose levels of DIVA in CF subjects who have a gating (G) variant and were previously taking a stable dose of
IVA. Randomization was stratified by ppFEV1 value at screening (<70 versus ≥70).
Figure 34. Study Design, Study 561-101

Source: Figure 9-1 CSR VX18-561-101
Abbreviations: D-IVA, deutivacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; q12h, every 12 hours; qd, once daily

Test Drug
Study drug was administered orally twice daily, with a fat-containing meal or snack, consistent
with the recommendations for administration of KALYDECO.
D-IVA
Four dose levels of D-IVA (25, 50, 150, and 250 mg) were evaluated to provide a broad range of
exposure for exposure-response analyses. The original protocol included 25 mg QD D-IVA and
50 mg QD D-IVA treatment groups; both treatment groups were discontinued at the Applicant’s
discretion. 44 subjects had been enrolled and randomized to the 5 treatment groups at that point.
198
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
A D-IVA dose regimen of 150 mg QD was predicted to provide exposure similar to IVA 150 mg
q12h. The 250 mg QD dose of D-IVA was included to provide a range of exposures to enable a
more robust exposure-response analysis.
IVA
The IVA dose regimen of 150 mg q12h is the approved regimen for treatment of CF in subjects
aged 6 years and older who have one variant in the CFTR gene that is responsive to IVA based
on clinical and/or in vitro assay data.
Samples
Blood samples for plasma PK analysis of D-IVA, IVA, and their relevant metabolites were
collected in all subjects at predose and at 4 hours after dosing on Day 1 following morning dose,
at predose on Weeks 2, 4, 8, and 12. In a subset of subjects (up to approximately 20 subjects),
additional samples were collected at the Week 4 visit at 1, 2, 4, and 6 hours after morning dose.
Efficacy and Pharmacodynamic Endpoints
Primary Efficacy Endpoint
Absolute change from baseline in percent predicted forced expiratory volume in 1 second
(ppFEV1) from baseline at Week 12
Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
Absolute change from baseline in SwCl concentrations at Week 12
Results
Pharmacokinetics Results
The mean observed trough concentrations across visits of D-IVA, IVA and their respective
metabolites are presented in Table 113. Based on the observed Ctrough values, steady state
exposures of D-IVA and M1-D-IVA were achieved by Day 15. Based on a comparison of mean
Ctrough values, exposures of D-IVA at the 250 mg QD dose were similar to IVA 150 mg q12h,
while exposures of M1-D-IVA were lower than the exposures of M1-IVA.

Efficacy Results
The analysis of the primary (ppFEV1) and the secondary (SwCl) efficacy endpoints is presented
in Table 114. The point estimate showed numerically greater reduction of SwCl from baseline at
Week 12 for D-IVA 250 mg QD than D-IVA 150 mg QD. The absolute changes in ppFEV1 and
the absolute changes in SwCl at Week 12 for D-IVA 150 mg QD, D-IVA 250 mg QD, and IVA
150 mg q12h were similar to IVA baseline (95% CIs include 0).
The point estimate for the within-treatment SwCl change at Week 12 for D-IVA 250 mg QD was
lower than at IVA baseline, while the point estimate for the within-treatment SwCl change at
Week 12 for D-IVA 150 mg QD was higher than at IVA baseline.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Conclusions
Exposures of D-IVA and M1-D-IVA in CF subjects with gating variants were generally similar
to exposures seen in healthy subjects administered the same doses of D-IVA. Mean Ctrough of DIVA following 250 mg QD regimen was similar to mean Ctrough of IVA following 150 mg q12h
dose in CF subjects with gating variants. Exposures of IVA and its metabolites were consistent
with historical exposures.
There was no meaningful difference regarding ppFEV1 changes from baseline (primary efficacy
endpoint) between 150 mg QD and 250 mg QD D-IVA dosing groups when compared to IVA
150 mg Q12h group. However, 250 mg D-IVA group demonstrated a numerically greater
reduction of SwCl from baseline (secondary endpoint) than 150 mg D-IVA group. Therefore, the
dose selection of carrying 250 mg D-IVA QD into phase 3 studies is reasonable.

14.2.6. Pediatric Pharmacokinetics and Safety Trial
14.2.6.1. Trial VX21-121-105 (Trial 105)
Title
A Phase 3 Study Evaluating the Pharmacokinetics, Safety, and Tolerability of
Vanzacaftor/Tezacaftor/Deutivacaftor Triple Combination Therapy in Cystic Fibrosis Subjects 1
Through 11 Years of Age
Trial Period
June 21, 2022 to November 27, 2023
Objective
Part A
Primary Objectives
•

To evaluate the PK of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites when dosed in TC

•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA

Part B
Primary Objective
•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA through Week 24

Secondary Objectives
•

To evaluate the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA through Week 24

•

To evaluate the PK of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites
201

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Trial Design
This is a 2-part (Parts A and B), multicohort, multicenter trial evaluating the PK, safety,
tolerability, and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC therapy in CF subjects 1 through 11 years of
age (inclusive) with at least one triple combination responsive (TCR) variant in the CFTR gene.
This interim analysis report includes final data from subjects 6 through 11 years of age enrolled
in Cohorts A1 and B1. Cohorts A2 and B2 will evaluate subjects 2 through 5 years of age
(inclusive). Cohorts A3 and B3 will evaluate subjects 1 to less than 2 years of age.
Figure 35 shows the trial design of Trial VX21-121-105. All subjects in Cohort A1 received the
same dose (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg QD) without body weight adjustment for 22
days. Subjects who participated in Cohort A1 were permitted to participate in Cohort B1 (if they
met eligibility criteria). A review of safety, tolerability, and available PK data was completed by
an internal team after Cohort A1 completed treatment to determine the doses and weight cutoffs
for Cohort B1. The dose studied in Cohort B1 was adjusted by body weight (i.e., 30 kg was used
as a cutoff, see Table 115).
In Part B, subjects who were receiving stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment had the Run-in Period
waived and entered the Treatment Period within 28 days following the Screening Visit. Subjects
who were not on stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment entered the 4-week Run-in Period and received
ELX/TEZ/IVA.
Subjects who were eligible were offered the opportunity to enroll in an optional open-label
extension safety trial evaluating VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA.

Cohort B1
In Cohort B1, 78 subjects enrolled and were on stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment for at least 4
weeks prior to receiving VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA on Day 1. Overall, 78 subjects received at least one
dose of trial drug in the Treatment Period, 77 (98.7%) subjects completed trial drug treatment,
and 78 (100%) subjects completed the trial. Most of subjects (91.0%) were White. A total of 44
(56.4%) subjects were male, and the mean (SD) age was 9.1 (1.7) years at Day 1. Overall, 37
(47.4%) subjects had the F/F genotype, 24 (30.8%) subjects had the F/MF genotype, and the rest
of the 17 (21.8%) subjects had another eligible genotype.
Trough concentrations for VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites were consistent across
visits. The mean observed trough concentrations of VNZ, TEZ, M1-TEZ, D-IVA, M1-D-IVA,
and M6-D-IVA in CF subjects 6 through 11 years of age in Cohort B1 (Table 118) were similar
to the values observed in CF subjects ≥12 years of age (Table 117).

Conclusions
Following the proposed pediatric dose in Cohort B1 in children 6 through 11 years of age
weighing ≥40 kg (VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg QD), the mean observed trough
concentrations of VNZ, TEZ, M1-TEZ, D-IVA, and M1-D-IVA were comparable to the values
observed in CF subjects ≥12 years of age from pivotal phase 3 Trials 102 and 103. Following the
recommended pediatric dose in Cohort B1 in CF pediatric subjects 6 through 11 years of age
weighing <40 kg (VNZ 12 mg/TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg QD), the mean observed trough
concentrations of VNZ, TEZ, M1-TEZ, D-IVA, and M1-D-IVA were at the lower end of the
range for each of the analytes (except for M1-TEZ which is similar) observed in CF subjects ≥12
years of age from pivotal phase 3 Trials 102 and 103.
There was a small numerical reduction of SwCl ( 8 nmol/L) from baseline (defined as at the
end of ELX/TEZ/IVA run-in treatment period) throughout 24-week treatment following the
proposed pediatric doses in children 6 through 11 years of age.

trials is listed in Table 119. Method validation for VNZ in plasma (P145, O403), unbound VNZ
in plasma (S440), VNZ in urine (R240), VNZ and TEZ in plasma (T008), D-IVA in plasma
(O085, O248, N104), and unbound D-IVA in plasma (S438) are adequate.

Analytical Method for VNZ in Plasma: Report # P145 (Low Range Method)
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of VNZ in dipotassiumethylenediaminetetraacetic acid (K2-EDTA) human plasma samples via liquid chromatography–
mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) Detection and was used in multiple phase 1 studies and phase 2
dose-ranging study (Study 101). The method was precise, accurate, sensitive, and selective with
a calibration range of 5.00 to 5000 ng/mL for VNZ. The analytes were stable under laboratory
analysis conditions. The long-term stability was 428 days (at ­20˚C and ­70˚C) for VNZ.
Analytical Method for VNZ in Plasma: Report # O403
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of VNZ in K2-EDTA
human plasma samples via LC-MS/MS Detection and was used in Study 001. The method was
precise, accurate, sensitive, and selective with a calibration range of 50.0 to 50000 ng/mL for
VNZ. The analytes were stable under laboratory analysis conditions. The long-term stability was
204 days (­20˚C and ­70˚C) for VNZ.
Analytical Method for VNZ, TEZ, and M1-TEZ in Plasma: Report # T008
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of VNZ, TEZ, and M1TEZ in K2-EDTA human plasma samples via LC-MS/MS Detection and was used in many phase
208
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
1 studies, pivotal phase 3 trials (Trials 102 and 103), and a pediatric PK and safety trial (Trial
105). The method was precise, accurate, sensitive, and selective with a calibration range of 5.00
to 5000 ng/mL for VNZ and 10.0 to 10000 ng/mL for TEZ and M1-TEZ. The analytes were
stable under laboratory analysis conditions. The long-term stability was 275 days (-at ­70˚C) for

Analytical Method for VNZ in Urine: Report # R240
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of VNZ in human
stabilized urine via LC-MS/MS Detection and was used in mass-balance Study 004. The method
was precise, accurate, sensitive, and selective with a calibration range of 1.0 to 1000 ng/mL for
VNZ. The analytes were stable under laboratory analysis conditions. The long-term stability was
96 days (at ­20˚C and ­70˚C) for VNZ.
Analytical Method for Unbound VNZ in Plasma: Report # S440
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of unbound VNZ in
human plasma/phosphate-buffered saline (PBS) (1:1, v/v) samples via LC-MS/MS Detection and
was used in dedicated hepatic impairment Study 008. The method was precise, accurate,
sensitive, and selective with a calibration range of 0.1 to 100 ng/mL for VNZ. The analytes were
stable under laboratory analysis conditions. The long-term stability in human plasma/PBS (1:1,
v/v) was 21 days (­80˚C) for VNZ.
Analytical Method for D-IVA and M1-D-IVA in Plasma: Report #O085
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of D-IVA and M1-DIVA in K2-EDTA human plasma samples via LC-MS/MS Detection and was used in all clinical
trials that analyzed D-IVA concentrations except one phase 1 study (Study 561-001). The
method was precise, accurate, sensitive, and selective with a calibration range of 2.00 to
2000 ng/mL for D-IVA and M1-D-IVA. The analytes were stable under laboratory analysis
conditions. The long-term stability was 342 days (at ­80˚C) for D-IVA and M1-D-IVA.
Analytical Method for D-IVA and M1-D-IVA in Plasma: Report #O248
An analytical method was developed and validated for the determination of D-IVA and M1-DIVA in K2-EDTA human plasma samples via LC-MS/MS Detection and was used in a phase 1
study (Study 561-001). The method was precise, accurate, sensitive, and selective with a
calibration range of 2.00 to 2000 ng/mL for D-IVA and M1-D-IVA. The analytes were stable
under laboratory analysis conditions. The long-term stability was 33 days (at ­20˚C and ­80˚C)
for D-IVA and M1-D-IVA.

Confidence intervals = estimate ± 1.96 × SE
Parameters estimated in the log-domain were back-transformed for clarity
Abbreviations: CI, confidence intervals; CV, coefficient of variation; IIV, interindividual variability; RSE, relative standard error;
SD, standard deviation; SE, standard error

The FDA’s Assessment
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analyses are considered acceptable for the purpose of
supporting analyses objectives. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no
significant discordance identified.

Table 126. Data Structure of TEZ Population PK Analysis, Report T271
General Information
Details
Objectives of PPK analysis
• Characterize the PK disposition of TEZ in subjects 6 years of
age and older using population methodology;
• Quantify the effects of covariates, including body weight and
CFTR genotype, as sources of PK variability in TEZ;
• Summarize predicted AUC0-24,ss for TEZ from the studied
dosing regimen for phase 3 subjects due to sparse PK
sampling;
• Evaluate if a 40 kg weight cutoff for all studied ages resulted
in similar exposures to adults;
­ Simulate TEZ exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dosing
regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in children 6 through 11
years with CF;
­ Simulate TEZ exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dosing
regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in adolescents 12- 17y
with CF;

inform dosing for adults with CF with moderate hepatic
impairment (MHI); and
• Simulate exposures of TEZ for different dosing regimens to
inform dosing for children 6 through 11 years coadministered
a strong or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor.

Analysis based on simulation
(optional)

Summary
• Simulations based on the final population
PK model for TEZ supported the
justification of candidate weight-based
dosing regimens of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC in
CF patients ≥6 years of age with a 40 kg
weight cutoff (Table 8; and Figures 14, 15,
S66, S71, Report T271).
• Additional weight cutoffs were also
evaluated:
­ 35 kg: Table S3; and Figures S60 and
S72, Report T271
­ 30 kg: Table S6; and Figures S63 and

Acceptability
(FDA’s Comments)
Acceptable

• Simulations based on the final population
PK models showed exposures of TEZ and
M1-TEZ in adult CF subjects with MHI and
no TEZ dose adjustment were in the same
range as subjects without MHI (Figure S74,
Report T271). This result supports that,
based on PK, no TEZ dose adjustment was
needed for adult CF subjects with MHI.
• Simulations based on the final population
PK models for TEZ and M1-TEZ supported
the justification of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dose
adjustment regimens with reduced daily
dose and/or dosing frequency for children 6
through 11 years with CF in the presence of
coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figure
S76 and S88, Report T271).

Details
Objectives of PPK analysis
• Characterize the PK disposition of M1-TEZ in subjects 6 years of age
and older using population methodology;
• Quantify the effects of covariates, including body weight and CFTR
genotype, as sources of PK variability in TEZ and M1-TEZ;
• Summarize AUCss, 0−24 for M1-TEZ from the studied dosing
regimen for phase 3 subjects due to sparse PK sampling;

14.5.1.5. M1-D-IVA and M6-D-IVA Population PK Analysis
(Report T406)
Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analyses are considered acceptable for the purpose of
supporting the analyses objectives. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the clinical
pharmacology reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
Population Pharmacokinetic Assessment Summary
Table 143. Data Structure of M1-D-IVA and M6-D-IVA Population PK Analysis, Report T406
General Information
Details
Objectives of PPK analysis • To characterize the PK disposition of M1-D-IVA and M6-D- IVA (D-IVA
metabolites) in subjects 6 years and older using population
methodology;
• To quantify the sources of variability;
• To summarize predicted AUC0­24h from the studied dosing regimen for
phase 3 subjects due to sparse PK sampling;
• To evaluate if a 40 kg weight cutoff for all studied ages resulted in
similar exposures to adults;
­ To simulate D-IVA and its metabolites exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/DIVA TC dosing regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in children 6
through 11 years with CF;
­ To simulate D-IVA and its metabolites exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/DIVA TC dosing regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in adolescents 12
through 17 years with CF;

Study included
D-IVA Dose(s) included
Population included

• To simulate exposures of D-IVA and its metabolites to inform dosing
and/or weight bounds for weight cutoffs other than 40 kg for subjects ≥6
years of age;
• To simulate exposures of D-IVA for different dosing regimens to inform
dosing for CF subjects with MHI;
• To simulate exposures of D-IVA for different dosing regimens to inform
dosing for children 6 through 11 years with CF coadministered a strong
or moderate cytochrome P4503A4 (CYP3A4) inhibitor
VX18-121-101, VX20-121-102, VX20-121-103, VX21-121-105, VX17-561001, VX18-561-101, VX16-770-018
25 mg, 50 mg, 100 mg, 125 mg, 150 mg, 250 mg QD (Table 5.4, Report
T406)

14.5.2. Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic
Modeling Review
Executive Summary
The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant’s PBPK modeling
analyses to predict the following:
(1) The DDI effect of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil)
on the PK of single and multiple doses of VNZ and D-IVA in adults.
(2) The DDI effect of strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers on the PK of multiple doses of
VNZ and D-IVA in adults.
(3) The DDI effect of strong and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on the PK of multiple doses of
VNZ and D-IVA in children aged 6 through 11 years.
(4) To determine dose adjustment regimens when using the to-be marketed FDC tablet (VNZ
Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of moderate or strong CYP3A
inhibitors in adults.
(5) The DDI effect of VNZ and D-IVA on CYP2C9 substrates.
The Divisions of Inflammation and Immune Pharmacology and Pharmacometrics have reviewed
the PBPK submission (Reports T183, T322, T333, response to Information Requests, August 23,
2024, and September 6, 2024, SDN 0019 and 0022, Module 1.11.3) to conclude the following:
•

Simulations predicted 2.4- to 3.9-fold increase in the AUC of VNZ when single or multiple
doses of VNZ was coadministered with multiple doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor.

•

Simulations predicted 2.9- to 4.8-fold increase in area under the concentration-time estimated
to infinity (AUCinf) and 3.1- to 5.1-fold increase in AUC0­24,ss of D-IVA when a single dose
or multiple doses of D-IVA was coadministered, respectively, with a moderate CYP3A
inhibitor.

•

Simulations predicted 82% and 90% reduction in VNZ and D-IVA exposure, respectively,
when coadministered with rifampin, a strong CYP3A inducer. Efavirenz, a moderate CYP3A
inducer, was predicted to decrease the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA by 69% and 73%,
respectively.

•

Simulation predicted increase in VNZ Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss by approximately 10- and 11fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg, and 10- and 9.5-fold in children 6
through 11 years weighing <40 kg, respectively, when multiple doses of VNZ were
coadministered with itraconazole, a strong CYP3A inhibitor. Similarly, D-IVA Cmax,ss and
AUC0­24,ss is predicted to increase by 7.4- and 14-fold in children 6 through 11 years
weighing ≥40 kg, and 6.8- and 14-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing <40 kg,
respectively, when D-IVA was coadministered with itraconazole.

•

Simulation predicted increase in VNZ Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss by 2.5- and 2.6-fold in children 6
through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg, and 2.5- and 2.6-fold in children 6 through 11 years
299

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
weighing <40 kg when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with fluconazole, a
moderate CYP3A inhibitor. The increase in D-IVA Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss when
coadministered with fluconazole was predicted to be 2.1- and 3.2-fold, respectively, in
children 6 through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg or <40 kg.
•

The predicted increases in AUC and Cmax with CYP3A inhibitors were similar between
children aged 6 through 11 years and adults at the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ and DIVA, assuming the exposure levels of CYP34 inhibitors are similar between adults and
pediatrics.

•

PBPK simulations in adults support reduction in the therapeutic dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
when coadministered with CYP3A4 inhibitors as follows:
– When coadministered with a strong CYP3A inhibitor reduce dose from two FDC tablets
(b) (4)
of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet once weekly
– When coadministered with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor reduce dose from two FDC
tablets of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet every other
(b) (4)
day
.

•

PBPK simulations did not rule out the DDI risk for clinical inhibition of D-IVA with a
sensitive CYP2C9 substrate (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide)

PBPK Model for Tezacaftor
The PBPK model for TEZ was reviewed under the original NDA 210491. The analysis was
considered adequate to predict the extent of the interaction effect with a moderate CYP3A
inhibitors under multiple-dose TEZ. Tezacaftor steady-state AUC and Cmax were predicted to
increase 2.1-fold and 1.7-fold, respectively, when the moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor fluconazole is
coadministered (NDA 210491, Clinical Pharmacology and Biopharmaceutics Review(s), Section
4). In a clinical DDI study with TEZ and the strong CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole, the observed
increase in AUC in the presence of itraconazole was 4-fold. The Applicant has prioritized
matching VNZ and D-IVA exposures over matching TEZ exposure for dose adjustments to
ensure exposures remain within the range of the therapeutic regimen. The reason for this
approach is that VNZ and D-IVA exposures were substantially increased by CYP3A inhibitors
as compared to TEZ. Therefore, dose adjustment of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA will be by the same
percentage. This rationale was considered acceptable by the review team.
PBPK Model for Vanzacaftor
Background
VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA are mainly metabolized by CYP3A4/5. In recombinant human CYPs
system, the remaining percentages of VNZ in CYP3A4 and CYP3A5 incubates were 43% and
81%, respectively, suggesting CYP3A4 and CYP3A5 may be the CYP isozymes involved in
VNZ metabolism (Study N295).
VNZ showed inhibition of CYP2B6 (IC50=14.2 µM), CYP2C8 (IC50=18.1 µM), and CYP2C9
(IC50=18.4 µM) in vitro (Study N189). At clinically relevant exposures, VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA
300
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
and their major metabolites showed low potential for CYP induction in human hepatocyte in
vitro assessments using mRNA as endpoint (Study 661-006 and Study CNCT-1005)
The Madin-Darby canine kidney and Caco-2 cells permeability results indicated VNZ is a low
permeable compound (Reports 280 and 327) and D-IVA exhibited moderate permeability in
wild-type Madin-Darby canine kidney II cells (Report CTP-656 DM-090). In vitro, VNZ was not
a substrate of efflux or hepatic uptake transporters, and it showed low potential to inhibit organic
anion transporter (OAT)P1B1 (IC50=45.1 µM, R =1.008), and OATP1B3 (IC50=61.7 µM,
R =1.006), but it may inhibit breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) (IC50=0.252 µM, R =532),
P-glycoprotein (P-gp) (IC50=3.8, R =35), and OAT1 (IC50=0.42 µM, R =0.8) (see Section 8.2).
The plasma protein binding, mainly to albumin, for VNZ and D-IVA is >99% (Reports N355,
M108, and T235).
Human mass balance data of VNZ (Study 004) showed that mean AUC0­inf value for unchanged
VNZ in plasma was approximately 77% of circulating TRA as determined by the AUC ratio
after a single dose of 14C-VNZ 20 mg/200 μCi administered as an oral solution. Mean recovery
of TRA was 0.502% in urine and 91.6% of the radioactive dose recovered in feces,
demonstrating minimal renal excretion and major fecal excretion. VNZ accounted for most of the
total circulating radioactivity in plasma (mean =96.2%) without major circulating metabolites.
VNZ is mainly cleared by metabolism in humans with unchanged VNZ recovered in excreta
accounted for less than 10% of the administered radioactive dose. The most abundant metabolite
in feces was M6-VNZ, which accounted for 26.1% of the administered radioactive dose followed
by M55-VNZ, M19-VNZ, M57-VNZ, and M9-VNZ, which accounted for 7.4%, 6.9%, 6.9%,
and 6.8% of the administered dose, respectively. The mean t1/2, clearance (CL)/F and Vz/F of
VNZ in plasma were 60.8 hr, 0.596 L/hr and 50.6 L, respectively. The distribution to blood was
56%.
Methods
Software
Simcyp® Version 21 (Simcyp Ltd, A Certara Company) was used for PBPK analyses by the
Applicant and the review team.
Model Development
The VNZ base model was constructed using the available in vitro ADME data and clinical data
(Study 007; itraconazole DDI study). The fraction of VNZ escaping gut extraction (Fg) and
fraction of VNZ dose absorbed (Fa) were predicted to be 0.98 and 0.90, respectively, by the firstorder absorption model, and bioavailability (F) was estimated to be 0.88. The absorption rate
constant (Ka) for VNZ was optimized to 0.91 h-1.
The central volume of distribution and CL were estimated using the full PBPK model and PK
data from Study 007 in which a single dose of 5 mg VNZ Form A was administered alone or in
the presence of itraconazole. The fraction of VNZ metabolized by CYP3A4 (fm CYP3A4) was
optimized to 0.95 based on in vitro human CYP reaction phenotyping studies (Study N295, % of
VNZ remaining in CYP3A4 occupant was 43%) and the clinical itraconazole DDI data (Study
007, fm =0.71).
301
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
In response to FDA’s request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted
sensitivity analysis of CYP3A4 fm values in the range of 60% to 95% to evaluate the impact of
uncertainty of the CYP3A4 fm value on the DDI effect of CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figure 87). Using
a CYP3A4 fm value of 0.95, the predicted AUClastR value was 3.2 following VNZ administration
with and without itraconazole for 14 days, which is at the lower limit of the observed AUClast R
(90%CI) of 3.49 (3.22, 3.78). Moreover, CYP3A4 fm of 0.95 predicted a AUCinf R of 10.5
following VNZ administration with and without itraconazole for 65 days. On the other hand, the
AUC ratios of VNZ were relatively insensitive to the CYP3A4 fm values in the range of 60% to
95% for moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g., fluconazole). For example, the range of the AUCinf
R for VNZ over the considered fm values was approximately 1.6 (Figure 88).

with itraconazole was 0.13 L/h, while the observed CL/F in Study 007 was 0.124 L/h. The blood
to plasma ratio of 0.588 is comparable to the distribution to blood of 56% obtained from the
mass balance Study 004.

Based on in vitro studies, VNZ is not a substrate of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, or OATP1B3. VNZ

assessment on CYP2C9 substrates (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide).
Model Validation
Clinical PK and DDI studies used to support VNZ model development, and validation are listed
in Table 162.
Vanzacaftor was provided in two forms in the clinical program, Form A and Form D. Form D
has had 50% bioavailability of Form A based on relative bioavailability results. To simulate the
PK profiles of VNZ Form D that was used in phase 3 studies in FDC, half the clinical dose was
used during simulations (i.e., if the clinical dose of VNZ is 20 mg, simulations were conducted
with 10 mg dose to capture the PK profile of 20 mg).
In response to FDA’s request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted
additional validation of the predictive performance of the PBPK models of VNZ and D-IVA (and
its metabolites) for multiple-dose PK in subjects with cystic fibrosis using FDC tablet from
Studies 102 and 103 (Table 162). Additional validation for VNZ PBPK model was performed by
the review team using PK data obtained following the administration of single or multiple doses
of VNZ Form A or Form D.
All simulation were conducted in adult healthy population with VNZ administered in fed state.
The fixed-dose combination drug is recommended to be taken with food. In DDI simulations
with itraconazole, VNZ was administered at the same time in fed stated. Simulations conducted
with VNZ administered in fed state 1 hour following the administration of itraconazole in fasted
state showed similar PK parameters compared to when VNZ and itraconazole were administered
at the same time.

Perpetrator Models
The software’s default PBPK models of the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole (fasted
solution) and moderate CYP3A inhibitors fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil, were used
to predict the CYP3A inhibition effects on VNZ PK.
Model Application
•

To simulate the effect of the strong CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole on the PK of VNZ at
steady state in adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.

•

To simulate the effect of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and
verapamil) on the PK of VNZ following single-dose and at steady state, in adults and
children aged 6 through 11 years.

•

To determine dose adjustment regimens when using the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg
/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors in
adults and children 6 through 11 years of age.

Results
Model Validation
Evaluation of the Predictive Performance for PK Under Different Dosing Scenario
The VNZ PBPK model adequately described the VNZ plasma concentration-time profiles
(Figure 89) and PK parameters (AUClast and Cmax) following a single dose of 5 mg [Form A]
administered under fed condition with predicted/observed ratios within 0.8 to 1.25 (Table 163).
Further, the model adequately predicted the PK of VNZ in the presence of itraconazole up to 264
hours post VNZ coadministration (Figure 89), and the AUClast and Cmax ratio for the DDI with
itraconazole (Table 164).
Of note, in the clinical DDI study (Study 007), itraconazole (solution) was taken in the fasted
condition approximately 1 hour before VNZ dosing in the fed state on the day of
coadministration. Although the Applicant’s PBPK analyses allowed dosing of VNZ under fed
state and itraconazole under fasted state, the 1-hour staggering in drugs dosing was not applied.
The review team conducted simulations between VNZ and itraconazole with 1-hour staggering
between the two drugs, and the simulated PK profiles and DDI effects were similar to dosing
VNZ and itraconazole at the same time. Moreover, this reviewer conducted the simulation with
itraconazole administered on Day 15 and VNZ administered on Day 18 (after 72 hours) and the
results were comparable to same day administration.
Additional model validation was conducted using PK of VNZ administered as 10 mg (Form A,
separate tablet, Study 005) and 20 mg (Form A separate tablet, Study 003), which showed
306
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
predicted/observed ratios for AUClast and Cmax within 0.8 to 1.25 (Table 163, Figure 90).
Moreover, the review team conducted model validation using multiple dose PK data in healthy
subjects of VNZ from two cohorts in Study 006. The predicted/observed ratios for AUClast and
Cmax were within 0.8 to 1.25 for one cohort in Study 006 (Table 163).
It appears that the performance of VNZ PBPK model to predict the PK data obtained from
healthy subjects (e.g., Studies 007, 005, and 006) is different to certain extent from the
predictability of PK data from subjects with CF (e.g., Studies 102 and 103). Of note, VNZ model
prediction of PK data from subjects with CF is around unity, compared to a wide prediction
performance (e.g., predicted/observed ratios range from 0.8 to 1.32) using PK data from healthy
subjects.

Model Application
Predict DDI With Single Dose and The proposed Therapeutic Dose of VNZ With CYP3A
Inhibitors and Inducers
VNZ PBPK model was used to simulate the effect of multiple doses of strong and moderate
CYP3A4 inhibitors on the PK of single and multiple doses of VNZ. Simulation results predicted
10.5-fold increase in the AUCinf, and a small change was predicted in Cmax (1.2-fold) of VNZ
following the administration of 5 mg single dose with multiple doses of itraconazole (Table 165).
Further, AUCinf of VNZ was predicted to increase by approximately 2.4- to 3.9-fold with
minimal change in Cmax (1.2-fold) when VNZ is coadministered as a single dose with multiple
doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor (Table 165).
The Applicant proposed to use the predicted exposure change after single dose administration of
VNZ to determine the DDI dose adjustment with CYP3A inhibitors. In response to FDA’s
request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted PBPK simulations to
predict AUC0­24h,ss and Cmax,ss ratios for the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ (20 mg, Form D,
309
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
once daily for 40 weeks) at steady state in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A
inhibitors. Due to VNZ concentration accumulation after multiple doses, the predicted ratios of
Cmax,ss at steady state were higher than the ratios of Cmax after a single dose. The predicted ratios
of AUC0­24h,ss at steady state for VNZ with and without multiple doses of CYP3A inhibitors were
similar to those from the single dose simulations based on AUCinf. Based on these results, the
performance of VNZ PBPK model to predict DDI appears compatible when VNZ was
administered as a single dose and at the proposed therapeutic doses. Therefore, the predicted
AUC ratios after single dose administration of VNZ can be used to determine dose adjustment
regimens when using the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the
presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors.

Comparison of Predicted DDI Ratios With CYP3A4 Inhibitors Between Adult and Pediatric (6
though 11 years) Populations at the Proposed Therapeutic Doses of VNZ
The exposure changes of VNZ when coadministered with strong or moderate CYP3A inhibitors
in adults were used to determine the DDI dose adjustment in children 6 through 11 years of age.
This approach assumes the effect of these CYP3A inhibitors on the PK of VNZ in this pediatric
age group are comparable to those in adults. To support this assumption, the pediatric population
model should be able to describe the PK of VNZ in children and the predicted DDI ratios with
CYP3A inhibitors in adults and pediatrics should be similar.
In response to FDA’s request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant used the
PK data of VNZ from Trial 105 to demonstrate the predictive PK performance of the pediatric

Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
PBPK model for children 6 through 11 years of age, categorized by body weight (<40 kg or
≥40 kg). Pediatric subjects with cystic fibrosis in Cohort B1 from Trial 105 received a weightbased dosing of the triple combination (TC) as follows:
•

For pediatric <40 kg, the TC was given as VNZ 12 mg [Form D] /TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg
administrated once daily for 24 weeks.

•

For pediatric ≥40 kg, the TC was given as VNZ 20 mg [Form D] /TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA
250 mg administrated once daily for 24 weeks.

The pediatric model adequately described VNZ plasma concentration-time profiles from Trial
105 (Figure 91) and PK parameters (AUC0­24,ss and Cmax,ss) with predicted/observed ratios
marginally at acceptable limit of 0.8 to 1.25 for pediatrics ≥40 kg, while the ratios for pediatrics
<40 kg were considerably above the acceptable limit (Table 166). Although the observed PK
data from Trial 105 were overpredicted by the PBPK model in pediatric subjects <40 kg, most of
the observed data is contained between the 5th and 95th percentile of the simulated PK profile of
VNZ in pediatric 6 through 11 years weighing <40 kg (Figure 91). Overall, the comparison of
the observed and predicted pediatric steady-state PK parameters of VNZ appears reasonable due
to the limited PK data of VNZ in pediatric subjects at the time of NDA submission in addition to
the high variability observed in the PK data from Trial 105.

Afterward, the Applicant used the pediatric PBPK model to predict the effects of

moderate CYP3A inhibitor fluconazole in this pediatric group and in adults is provided in Table
167. Note that the doses of the CYP3A inhibitors were reduced in the pediatric group to match
the exposure levels observed in healthy adult subjects at steady state. Overall, the fold changes in
AUC and Cmax with CYP3A inhibitors were similar between pediatric 6 through 11 years and
adult populations at the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ, assuming the exposure levels of the
CYP3A inhibitor are similar between adult and pediatric populations.

PBPK Model for D-IVA and Metabolites
Method
Software
Simcyp® Version 21 (Simcyp Ltd, A Certara Company) was used for PBPK analyses by the
Applicant and the review team.
Model Development
D-IVA
The D-IVA PBPK model consists of a first-order absorption model constructed using available
in vitro ADME data and drug interaction with itraconazole (Study VX18-445-006, NDA
212273). The absorption of D-IVA was assumed to be complete (Fa =1), and the absorption rate
constant for D-IVA was assumed to be similar to ivacaftor (Ka =0.546 h-1). (See NDA 203188,
Clinical Pharmacology and Biopharmaceutics Review(s), Appendix 1). The Fg of D-IVA was
predicted to be 0.76.
D-IVA PK was described by a one-compartment popPK model with seven transit compartments,
first-order absorption, and linear elimination (Report t406). A minimal PBPK model and single
adjusting compartment were used to estimate Vss =0.85 L/kg for D-IVA with Kin and Kout of 0.1
and 0.14 h-1, respectively.
Ivacaftor has a large Vz/F, suggesting extensive tissue distribution. The mean Vz/F of ivacaftor
after a single dose of 275 mg of ivacaftor in healthy subjects and subjects with CF in fed state
313
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
was 203 to 206 L and after multiple-dose administration of ivacaftor 250 mg q12h was 206 L in
healthy subjects in the fed state. Similarly, D-IVA showed large Vz/F. Values of 106 L and 250
L were reported when coadministered with and without itraconazole, respectively (Study VX18445-006), and a Vz/F =183 L was estimated by popPK analysis (Report t406). The geometric
mean predicted Vss for D-IVA when administered alone was 228 L calculated from CL and
Lambda z, which is comparable to the observed value in Study VX18-445-006. The predicted
geometric mean CL of D-IVA when coadministered with and without itraconazole was 11.83 L/h
and 1.53 L/h, respectively, which is comparable to the observed CL values of 11.9 L/h and 1.01
L/h, respectively, from Study VX18-445-006.
The fraction of D-IVA metabolized by CYP3A4 was optimized to 0.95 based on in vitro human
CYP reaction phenotyping studies (Report S286, % of D-IVA remaining in CYP3A4 occupant
was 6%) and the clinical itraconazole DDI data (Study VX18-445-006, fm =0.9). Sensitivity
analysis of CYP3A4 fm showed that there is a narrow range of CYP3A4 fm values that is required
to describe the clinical DDI data with itraconazole for D-IVA. For example, using CYP3A4 fm of
0.95 predicted an AUCinfR =12.4 following D-IVA administration with and without itraconazole
that was contained within the observed 90%CI of AUCinfR (8.72 – 14.1) (Figure 92).The
AUCinfR for D-IVA was relatively insensitive to CYP3A4 fm values in the range of 60% to 95%
for fluconazole. For example, the range of AUCinfR for D-IVA over the considered fm values
was approximately 1.8 to 2.9 for the moderate CYP3A inhibitor fluconazole (Figure 92).
The CLint of 24.2 μL/min/pmol for D-IVA by CYP3A was calculated using the retrograde
method based on estimated CLiv =7.1 L/h and fm =0.95. The additional clearance by human liver
microsomes was estimated to be 155.7 µL/min/mg protein. The PBPK model input parameters
for D-IVA are listed in Table 168.

At each CYP3A4 fm, CLint was calculated using the reverse transitional tool then simulation was conducted to predict the increase in
AUC of D-IVA following the administration of itraconazole or fluconazole 200 mg once daily for 10 days. D-IVA was administered as
single dose of 50 mg in fed state on Day 4 and 1 hours staggering following the administration of itraconazole or fluconazole.
Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; AUCinf, area under the concentration-time curve estimated to infinity;
CI, confidence interval; CYP, cytochrome P450; D-IVA, deutivacaftor; fm, fraction metabolized

The D-IVA inhibitory potential of CYP enzymes were included in the model. This is because in
vitro data showed that D-IVA inhibited CYP2C8 (IC50=9.5 µM), CYP2C9 (IC50=15.6 µM), and
CYP3A4 (IC50=15.7 µM, testosterone as a probe substrate) with R-value above the defined
criteria (Reports D-IVA DM-058, CPT-656-DM-085). Clinically, IVA did not significantly
affect the PK of rosiglitazone, a CYP2C8 substrate, and IVA and TEZ/IVA had no clinically
significant effect on midazolam exposures, CYP3A substrate (Study 770-010, NDA 203188 and
Study 661-006, NDA 210491). Based on in vitro inhibition parameters and the above clinical
DDI data, it is expected that D-IVA and its metabolites have low potential to mediate CYP
enzymes inhibition at clinically relevant concentrations, except that D-IVA may inhibit
CYP2C9.
There is no mass balance Study available for D-IVA. In mass balance Study 003 for ivacaftor
(NDA 203188), mean total recovery was 94.6%, of which 87.8% (mean) was excreted in feces
and 6.6% was excreted in urine. The amount of unchanged drug excreted in feces was only
2.52%, suggesting that most of the drug was excreted as metabolites.
Based on in vitro studies, D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are substrates of P-gp and BCRP. The model
did not include intestinal efflux transport-mediated disposition for D-IVA based on the
assumption of high oral absorption (as reported with ivacaftor) and approximately linear PK after
single dose. D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are P-gp inhibitors with IC50 of 3.83 and 9.86 µM,
315
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
respectively (see Section 8.2). However, when TEZ/IVA was coadministered with digoxin,
Study 661-006 (NDA 210491), a sensitive P-gp substrate, digoxin exposure increased by 1.3fold, indicating weak inhibition of P-gp by IVA (IC50=10.6 µM) which has similar P-gp
inhibition potency as D-IVA. Therefore, the DDI risk of D-IVA is predicted to be similar to
IVA. Moreover, D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are BCRP inhibitors with IC50 of 1.19 and 9.15 µM,
respectively (see Section 8.2).
Model Validation
Clinical PK and DDI studies used to support the model development and validation are listed in
Table 169. The PBPK models for D-IVA and its metabolites were developed and validated using
the PK data obtained from DDI with itraconazole (Study VX18-445-006, NDA 212273).
Study VX18-445-006 evaluated the effect of itraconazole on the PK of a single dose of
elexacaftor (ELX) 20 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 50 mg and relevant metabolites administered as
separate tablets. In period 1, ELX, TEZ, and D-IVA, were administered on Day 1 in fed state
followed by 14 to 21 washout period. In Period 2 (Day -4 to 7), itraconazole 200 mg was
administered in fasted from Day -3 to Day 6, followed by breakfast and administration of
ELX/TEZ/D-IVA on Day 1 (i.e., Day 4 of itraconazole administration).
All PBPK simulation were conducted in fed state with ELX/TEZ/D-IVA administered on Day 4
of itraconazole administration with 1 hours staggering where itraconazole was administered in
the fasted state.

Perpetrator Models
The software’s default PBPK models of the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole (fasted
solution) and moderate CYP3A inhibitors fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil were used
to predict the CYP3A inhibition effects on the PK of D-IVA.
Model Application
•

To simulate the effect of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and
verapamil) on the PK of D-IVA in adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.

•

To determine dose adjustment regimens when using the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg
/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors in
adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.

Results
Model Validation
Evaluation of the Predictive Performance for PK Under Different Dosing Scenario
The PBPK model for D-IVA adequately described the plasma concentration-time profiles of DIVA when D-IVA was administered with and without itraconazole in Study VX18-445-006
(Figure 93). In addition, the model adequately predicted the mean values for PK parameters
(AUCinf and Cmax) when a single 50 mg dose of D-IVA was administered alone and in the
presence of itraconazole with predicted/observed ratios marginally deviating from 0.8 to 1.25

In response to FDA’s request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted
additional validation of D-IVA PBPK model by comparing predicted versus observed steadystate PK parameters of D-IVA, M1-D-IVA, and M6-D-IVA in CF subjects from studies 102 and
103 when D-IVA was administered as FDC tablet (TEZ 100 mg/VNZ 20 mg Form D/D-IVA
250 mg) once daily in fed state (Table 172 and Figure 94). It appears that D-IVA model tends to
under predict the popPK estimates of AUClast from studies 102 and 103 following the
administration of 250 mg once daily. Similarly, the additional model validation, conducted by
the reviewer, showed that D-IVA model under predicted the observed AUClast from Study 006.
This bias for under prediction noted in the D-IVA model could be justified by the high variability
in observed and predicted PK data.
The review team acknowledges this limitation in the D-IVA PBPK model. We note, however,
this model limitation is not expected to have a significant impact on the overall application to
inform dose reduction when D-IVA is coadministered with CYP3A4 inhibitors, when
considering the constrains of DDI dose adjustment strategy (i.e., use of FDC formulation,
reducing number of tablets and/or dosing frequency).

Model Application
Predict DDI With Single Dose and the Proposed Therapeutic Dose of D-IVA With CYP3A
Inhibitors and Inducers
The D-IVA model was used to simulate the effect of multiple doses of strong and moderate
CYP3A4 inhibitors on the PK of single (50 mg) and multiple doses (250 mg once daily for 12
weeks) of D-IVA. Simulation results predicted 12.4-fold increase in the AUCinf, and 1.9-fold
increase in Cmax of D-IVA following the administration of a single 50 mg dose of D-IVA with
itraconazole. Further, simulations predicted 2.9- to 4.78-fold increase in AUCinf of D-IVA when
D-IVA was coadministered as a single dose with moderate CYP3A inhibitors (Table 173).
PBPK simulations to predict AUC0­24h,ss ratios for the proposed therapeutic doses of D-IVA
(250 mg once daily) at steady state in the presence of strong or moderate CYP3A inhibitors
showed similar increase in AUC0­24h,ss of D-IVA when compared to DDI ratios of single dose DIVA. Due to D-IVA concentration accumulation after multiple doses, the predicted ratios of
Cmax,ss at steady state were higher than the ratios of Cmax after a single dose (Table 173). The
predicted ratios of AUC0­24h,ss at steady state for D-IVA with and without multiple doses of
CYP3A inhibitor were similar to those from the single dose simulations based on AUCinf.

Comparison of Predicted DDI Ratios With CYP3A4 Inhibitors Between Adult and Pediatric (6
Through 11 Years) Populations at the Proposed Therapeutic Doses of D-IVA
Similar to analysis conducted for VNZ, the Applicant used the PK data of D-IVA from Trial 105
to demonstrate the predictive PK performance of the pediatric PBPK model for children 6
through 11 years of age, categorized by body weight (<40 kg or ≥40 kg).
The predicted PK parameters (AUC0­24,ss and Cmax,ss) for D-IVA and its metabolites were within
a ~2-fold range of the observed PK data from Trial 105 Cohort B1 due to the high variability in
PK data (Table 174). Generally, the observed data is contained between the 5th and 95th
percentile of the simulated PK profile of D-IVA in pediatric 6 through 11 years weighing
<40 kg; however, the model under predicted the PK profiles of D-IVA in pediatric 6 through 11
years weighing ≥40 kg (Figure 95). Overall, the comparison of the observed and predicted
pediatric steady-state PK parameters of D-IVA appears reasonable due to the limited PK data of
D-IVA in pediatric subjects at the time of NDA submission and the limitation noted in D-IVA
PBPK model, as previously discussed.

The Applicant has used the pediatric PBPK model to predict the effects of coadministration of

A comparison of predicted ratios of AUC0­24h,ss and Cmax,ss for D-IVA and its metabolites at

PBPK Modeling to Support Dose Adjustment of VNZ and D-IVA
Coadministration of TC therapy with CYP3A4 inhibitors resulted in a substantial decrease in CL/F
which in return increased the overall exposure of VNZ and D-IVA compared to subjects taking
TC therapy without coadministration of CYP3A4 inhibitors. PBPK simulations were performed
to evaluate two different dose adjustment regimens of VNZ and D-IVA when the FDC tablet (VNZ
Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) is coadministered with strong or moderate CYP3A
inhibitors in adults. Given that the predicted increase exposures in the presence of CYP3A
inhibitors were higher for VNZ and D-IVA compared to TEZ, and the constraints of using a FDC
tablet, the Applicant has prioritized matching VNZ and D-IVA exposures over matching TEZ
exposure for DDI dose adjustments to ensure exposures remain within the range of the therapeutic
regimen. Therefore, dose adjustment of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA will be by the same percentage.
Strong CYP3A Inhibitors
The Applicant has evaluated two dose adjustments, one FDC tablet once weekly and two FDC
tablets every other week, against the exposures at the therapeutic dose in the absence of CYP3A
inhibitors, two FDC tablets once daily. The simulated plasma concentration-time profiles of
VNZ and D-IVA are shown in Figure 96. The geometric fold change of simulated steady-state
VNZ and D-IVA exposures for the dose adjustment regimens in the presence of a strong CYP3A
inhibitor (itraconazole) relative to two FDC tablets once daily in the absence of a CYP3A
inhibitor are presented in Table 176. Alternative dosing regimens of VNZ (one FDC Q1W and
two FDC Q2W) coadministered with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors has maintained exposure within
or below the 5th and 95th percentiles determined from therapeutic dosing regimen of TC (two
FDC QD) in the absence of strong CYP3A3 inhibitors. The exposure to VNZ and D-IVA from
the two dose adjustment regimes in the presence of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors is within 1.5-fold
of VNZ and D-IVA exposures from the therapeutic dosing regimen in the absence of strong
CYP3A4 inhibiters (Table 176).
Moderate CYP3A4 Inhibitors
Two dose adjustments, one FDC tablet every other day and two FDC tablets on Day 1 and two
FDC tablets on Day 4 weekly, against the exposures at the therapeutic dose in the absence of

therapeutic dosing regimen 2FDC QD in the absence of moderate CYP3A4 inhibiters.

PopPK model-based simulations were performed to inform dose adjustments for adults and
children aged 6 through 11 years with CF that were coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors. For
these simulations, clearance values were adjusted for subjects receiving strong or moderate
CYP3A4 inhibitor based on changes in AUC predicted by the PBPK simulations (Table 165 and
Table 173). The potential dose adjustments for children aged 6 to11years coadministered VNZ
and D-IVA with CYP3A4 inhibitors aimed to provide exposures that are similar to VNZ and DIVA exposures without CYP3A4 inhibitors in adults with CF. Review of this model-based
analysis and final recommendations of DDI dose adjustment for pediatrics are presented in
Section 14.5.1 of the review.

Prediction of DDI Potential of VNZ and D-IVA as Victims for Moderate and Strong
CYP3A4 Inducers
In response to FDA’s information request (dated September 6, 2024), the Applicant provided
PBPK simulations to support the DDI management strategy of VNZ/D-IVA with moderate and
strong CYP3A inducers in the proposed label. Simulation showed that the exposure to VNZ and
D-IVA decreased significantly by 82% and 90%, respectively, when coadministered with
rifampin, a strong CYP3A inducer. Moreover, carbamazepine, a strong CYP3A inducer,
significantly decreased VNZ and D-IVA exposures by 57% and 76%, respectively (Table 177).
Simulation showed that the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA decreased by 69% and 73%,
respectively, when coadministered with efavirenz, a moderate CYP3A inducer.
In summary, moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducers significantly decreased the exposure to VNZ
and D-IVA, which may impact the efficacy of TC therapy in CF patients. Therefore,
coadministration of TC therapy with moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducers is not recommended.

Prediction of DDI Potential of VNZ and D-IVA as Inhibitors on CYP2C9 Substrates
In vitro data showed that D-IVA inhibited CYP2C8 (IC50=9.5 µM, R-value =1.05), CYP2C9
(IC50=15.6 µM, R-value =1.03), and CYP3A4 (IC50=15.7 µM, testosterone as a probe substrate,
R-value =1.03) with R-value above the defined criteria (Reports D-IVA DM-058, CPT-656-DM085). Clinically, IVA did not significantly affect the PK of rosiglitazone, a CYP2C8 substrate
(Study 770-010, NDA 203188) or midazolam, CYP3A substrate, (Study 661-006, NDA
210491)). Moreover, in vitro data showed that M1-D-IVA inhibited CYP2C9 (IC50=4.6 µM, Rvalue =1.09). Therefore, D-IVA and its metabolites have low potential to mediate CYP enzymes
inhibition at clinically relevant concentrations, except that D-IVA and M1-D-IVA may inhibit
330
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
CYP2C9. VNZ may have weak inhibition CYP2C9 (IC50=18.4 µM) (Study N189) with R-value
below the defined criteria.
In response to FDA’s information request (dated September 6, 2024), The Applicant provided
PBPK simulations for DDI of multiple-doses of VNZ and D-IVA as perpetrators (i.e., CYP2C9
inhibitors) with a CYP2C9 substrate (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide). Using in vitro determined
IC50 values, the inhibition of CYP2C9 by VNZ or D-IVA had no notable impact on the exposure
to warfarin and tolbutamide (Table 178). However, applying a minimal uncertainty factor of 3fold lower IC50 value for D-IVA, the risk for a clinical interaction could not be excluded (e.g.,
the predicted AUC R values of tolbutamide reached the threshold of 1.25). Therefore, the PBPK
analysis supports the Applicant’s proposed DDI management strategy of cautionary use or
monitoring when D-IVA is coadministered with CYP2C9 substrates, such as warfarin.

unfound fraction in the microsomes (fumic) of 0.167 (Report CTP-656 DM-085).
The in vitro CYP2C9 IC50 values for VNZ, D-IVA, and M1-D-IVA measured in human liver microsomes were corrected for
nonspecific binding in the assay to obtain free IC50 values (Reports DM-058, CTP-656 DM-085, S016, R015, and S079). The free
IC50 values were varied 3-fold from the measured value in both directions to incorporate the impact of any experimental uncertainty
in measurements.

metabolite to be included as an inhibitor. However, M6-D-IVA does not inhibit CYP2C9 in vitro and its absence is the model has no
impact on the outcome (Report CTP-656 DM-085).

Conclusions
VNZ AUC was predicted to increase by approximately 2.4- to 3.9-fold when single or multiple
doses of VNZ was coadministered with multiple doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor.
331
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Coadministration of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor with single dose VNZ increased VNZ Cmax by
1.2-fold, whereas VNZ Cmax,ss increased by 2.5- to 3.8-fold due to accumulation.
Simulations predicted 2.9- to 4.5-fold increase in AUCinf and 3.1- to 5.1-fold increase in D-IVA
AUC0­24,ss when D-IVA was coadministered as a single dose or multiple doses, respectively, with
a moderate CYP3A inhibitor. Coadministration of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor with single dose
D-IVA increased D-IVA Cmax by 1.7-fold, whereas D-IVA Cmax,ss increased by 2.3 to 3.4-fold
due to accumulation.
Simulation predicted increase in VNZ Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss by approximately 10- and 11-fold in
children 6 through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg, and 10-and 9.5-fold in children 6 through 11 years
weighing <40 kg, respectively, when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with a strong
CYP3A inhibitor. D-IVA geometric mean ratio of Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss by 7.36 and 14.3 (in
children 6 through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg) and 6.76 and 14 (in children 6 through 11 years
weighing <40 kg), respectively, when D-IVA was coadministered with itraconazole
Simulation predicted increase in VNZ geometric mean ratio of Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss by 2.54 and
2.64 (in children 6 through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg) and 2.49 and 2.6 (in children 6 through 11
years weighing <40 kg) when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with fluconazole, a
strong CYP3A4. The increase in D-IVA geometric mean ratio of Cmax,ss and AUC0­24,ss when
coadministered with fluconazole was predicted to be 2.1 and 3.17, respectively, in children 6
through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg or <40 kg.
PBPK simulations in adults support reduction in the therapeutic dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA when
coadministered with CYP3A4 inhibitors as follows:
•

When coadministered with a strong CYP3A inhibitor: reduce dose from two FDC tablets of
(b) (4)
(VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet once weekly

•

When coadministered with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor: reduce dose from two FDC tablets
(b) (4)
of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet every other day

Simulations predicted 82% and 90% reduction in VNZ and D-IVA exposure, respectively, when
coadministered with rifampin, a strong CYP3A inducer. In addition, simulations predicted 57%
and 76%, reduction in VNZ and D-IVA exposure, respectively, when coadministered with
carbamazepine, a strong CYP3A inducer. Efavirenz, a moderate CYP3A inducer, was predicted
to decrease the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA by 69% and 73%, respectively. Therefore,
simulations support that coadministration of TC therapy with moderate or strong CYP3A4
inducers is not recommended.
The DDI potential of D-IVA as inhibitor of CYP2C9 substrates (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide)
cannot be ruled out.

14.6. Pharmacogenetics
Not applicable.
