15. Study/Trial Design
15.1. Phase 3 Trials, Trials 102 and 103
Overview and Objectives
Trials VX20-121-102 (Trial 102) and VX20-121-103 (Trial 103) were phase 3, multicenter,
randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, controlled, noninferiority trials evaluating the safety
and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in subjects with CF 12 years of age and older.
Trial 102 Title: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-blind, Controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy
and Safety of VX-121 [VNZ] Combination Therapy [VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA] in Subjects with Cystic
Fibrosis who are Heterozygous for F508del and a Minimal Function Mutation (F/MF)
•

Trial dates: September 14, 2021 to November 21, 2023

•

Trial report date: April 5, 2024

Trial 103 Title: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-blind, Controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy
and Safety of VNZ Combination Therapy [VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA] in Subjects with Cystic Fibrosis
Who Are Homozygous for F508del, Heterozygous for F508del and a (F/G) or (F/ RF) Mutation,
or Have At Least 1 Other Triple Combination Responsive CFTR Mutation and No F508del
Mutation
•

Trial dates: October 27, 2021 to November 30, 2023

•

Trial report date: April 5, 2024

Overall, the two phase 3 trials were similar in design and were adequate and well controlled. The
general trial designs will be reviewed together with relevant differences noted.
Primary Objective
•

To evaluate the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in CF subjects

Secondary Objectives
•

To evaluate the safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA

•

To evaluate the pharmacokinetics (PK) of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA

Trial Design
The phase 3 trials included a 4-week (28 day) screening period followed by a 4-week (28 day)
run-in period during which all subjects received ELX 200 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg
q12h (the commercially approved dose) to establish a reliable on-treatment (ELX/TEZ/IVA)
baseline for the treatment period. Spirometry and SwCl assessments obtained during the run-in
period (Day -14) were obtained and used for stratification and randomization. Following
completion of the run-in period, the subjects were randomized 1:1 to the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
333
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
(treatment) arm or the ELX/TEZ/IVA (control) arm for a 52-week treatment period that included
in-person clinic visits on Day 14, Weeks 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, and 52 with intermittent telephone
contact in between scheduled visits. A follow-up safety visit occurred for any subject who
prematurely discontinued trial drug (28±7 days after the last dose of trial drug). Safety follow-up
visits were not required for subjects who completed the treatment period and transitioned within
28 days to one of the following: an open-label trial, another qualified trial run by the Applicant,
or a commercially available or managed access program-supplied program for another
medication from the Applicant. The trial schematic is shown below in Figure 99.
Figure 99. Pivotal Phase 3 Trial Schematic, Trial 102

Source: Applicant provided (Protocol VX20-121-102, Version 3.0. Figure 2-1).
Abbreviations: D-IVA, deutivacaftor; ELX, elexacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; N, number of subjects, TEZ, tezacaftor; VX121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor

Subjects were permitted to be rescreened once during which all screening assessments were
repeated with the exception of CFTR-genotyping and, if applicable, follicle-stimulating hormone
level and ophthalmologic examinations. Subjects were stratified by age at screening visit (<18
years versus ≥18 years of age), ppFEV1 determined during the Run-in Period (<70% versus
≥70%), SwCl determined during the Run-in Period (<30mmol/L versus ≥30mmol/L), and prior
use of a medication that affects CFTR processing or function (e.g., ELX/TEZ/IVA) use (yes
versus no).
Subjects who completed the 52-day treatment period were able to enroll in the open-label
extension study (Trial VX20-121-104), if eligible.
Trial Population
The trials enrolled adults and adolescents (12 years and older) with stable CF per the following
enrollment criteria and eligible CFTR genotypes as outlined in Table 179.

The additional key eligibility criteria are listed below:
Key Inclusion Criteria
•

Ages 12 years or older

•

Confirmed diagnosis of CF as determined by the investigator

•

CFTR genotype as reviewed in Table 179

•

Percent predicted forced expiratory volume in one second {Quanjer, 2012 #12}:
– Subjects receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA at screening: ≥40% and ≤90%
– Subjects not receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA at screening: ≥40% and ≤80%

•

Stable CF disease as judged by the investigator

Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730

Key Exclusion Criteria
•

History of comorbidity that might confound the results in the opinion of the investigator.
Including, but not limited to:
– Hepatic cirrhosis with portal hypertension, moderate hepatic impairment (Child Pugh
Score 7 to 9), severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh Score 10 to 15)
– Sold organ or hematological transplantation
– Alcohol or drug abuse in the past year
– Cancer except for squamous cell skin cancer, basal cell skin cancer, and Stage 0 cervical
carcinoma in situ

•

History of intolerance to trial drug that would pose additional risk to the subject in the
opinion of the investigator (e.g., subjects with a history of liver function test (LFT) elevations
requiring treatment interruption or discontinuation, allergy or hypersensitivity to the trial
drug)

•

Any of the following abnormal labs at screening:
– Hemoglobin <10 g/dL
– Total bilirubin ≥2 x upper limit of normal (ULN)
– Aspartate transaminase (AST), alanine transaminase (ALT), gamma-glutamyl transferase,
or alkaline phosphatase (ALP) ≥3 x ULN
– Abnormal renal function defined as glomerular filtrate rate ≤50 mL/min/1.73 m2
(Modification of Diet in Renal Disease Study Equation (Levey et al. 1999; Levey et al.
2006)) for subjects ≥18 years and ≤45 mL/min/1.73 m2 (Counahan-Barratt equation
(Counahan et al. 1976)) for subjects 12-17 years

•

An acute upper or lower respiratory tract infection, infectious pulmonary exacerbation (PEx)
of CF, or changes in therapy (including antibiotics) for sinopulmonary disease within 28 days
before the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA in the run-in period

•

Lung infection with organisms associated with a more rapid decline in pulmonary status
(including but not limited to Burkholderia cenocepacia, Burkholderia dolosa, and
Mycobacterium abscessus). For subjects who have had a history of a positive culture, the
investigator will apply the following criteria to establish whether the subject is free of
infection with such organisms:
– The subject has not had a respiratory tract culture positive for these organisms within the
12 months before the date of informed consent
– The subject has at least two respiratory tract cultures negative for these organisms within
the 12 months before the day of informed consent, with the first and last of these
separated by at least 3 months, and the most recent one within the 6 months before the
date of informed consent

The overall population for the phase 3 trials was reasonable and consistent with other similar
clinical development programs in the CF population. Note that subjects unable to tolerate
ELX/TEZ/IVA were excluded. This is a limitation of the trial design and may affect both the
overall generalizability and ability to assess the safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in patients unable to
tolerate ELX/TEZ/IVA, an area of unmet need in the CF population, as reviewed in
Section 7.7.1.
Trial Drug Interruption and Stopping Rules
Liver Function Tests
The protocol stated that the central laboratory would notify the medical monitor of ALT or AST
>3 x ULN and total bilirubin >2 x ULN. Subjects with new treatment-emergent ALT or AST
elevations of >3 x ULN, with or without total bilirubin >2 x ULN were followed closely with
confirmatory testing within 28 to 72 hours of the initial finding.
Trial drug administration was interrupted immediately (prior to confirmatory testing) if any of
the following criteria were met:
•

ALT or AST >8 x ULN

•

ALT or AST >5 x ULN for more than 2 weeks

•

ALT or AST >3 x ULN in association with total bilirubin >2 x ULN and/or clinical jaundice

A thorough investigation of potential causes was conducted, and the subject was monitored
closely for clinical progression.
Trial drug administration was discontinued if subsequent ALT or AST values confirmed the
initial evaluation that satisfied the interruption rule and no convincing alternative etiology (e.g.,
acetaminophen use, viral hepatitis, alcohol ingestion) was identified, regardless of whether the
transaminase levels improved. All subjects for whom treatment was discontinued for elevated
transaminases and bilirubin, had levels monitored closely until normalization or return to
baseline. If an alternative, reversible cause of transaminase elevation, with or without increased
bilirubin or clinical jaundice, was identified, trial drug administration was resumed once the
transaminases returned to baseline or were ≤2 x ULN, whichever was higher. Upon resumption,
transaminases and bilirubin were assessed weekly for 4 weeks. Trial drug was permanently
discontinued if a protocol-defined transaminase interruption threshold recurred within 4 weeks of
the rechallenge of the trial drug.

Rash
Trial drug dosing was interrupted for subjects who developed a generalized rash Grade 3 or
higher or a rash that was considered a serious adverse event (SAE). The investigators were
advised to consider additional evaluations and were able to reconsider resumption of trial drug if
considered clinically appropriate.
Other
In addition, subjects were discontinued from trial drug treatment if they met any of the following
criteria:
•

Had a screening CFTR genotype that does not confirm trial eligibility if a previous CFTR
genotype laboratory report was used to establish eligibility.

•

Became pregnant

•

Met any trial drug discontinuation criteria (reviewed above)

Subjects who withdrew before the first does of trial drug prior to Day 1 of the Treatment Period
were able to be replaced.
The stopping criteria outlined in the protocol’s was acceptable.
Dose and Duration of Treatment
The dosages evaluated in the phase 3 trials were as follows:
•

Active control: ELX 200 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg q12h

•

Treatment: VNZ 20 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/D-IVA 250 mg QD

The trial drug was administered orally with a fat-containing meal or snack. It was recommended
that the dose be taken within 30 mins of the start of the meal or snack.
If six hours or fewer had passed since the missed morning or evening dose, the subject was to
take the missed dose as soon as possible and continue on the original schedule. If more than 6
hours had passed since the missed morning dose, the subject was to take the missed dose as soon
as possible and not take the evening dose. If more than six hours had passed since the missed
evening dose, the subject should not take the missed doses. The next scheduled morning dose
was to be taken at the usual time. The morning and evening doses were not to have been taken at
the same time.
All subjects (and their parents/caregivers/companions), site personnel (including investigators,
site monitor, and trial team), and members of the Applicant’s trial team were blinded to the
treatment group. The Applicant’s trial team did not have access to the spirometry or SwCl results
after the first dose of the trial drug on Day 1 of the treatment period, with the exception of
spirometry related adverse event reporting. Individual SwCl results were also not disclosed to the
trial sites with the exception of the screening visit and Day -14 values. Subjects and their

Subjects remained on their stable standard-of-care CF regimen with the exception of any

Key Prohibited Medications
•

Moderate and strong CYP3A inducers (within 14 days before the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA

•

Non-Vertex medications that affect CFTR processing or function, investigational or

Vertex medications that affect CFTR processing or function, investigational or approved

Prior and Concomitant Medications
•

Subjects were to remain on stable treatment regimen for CF from 28 days before the Run-in
through completion of trial participation. Guidelines for stable CF treatment regimens
included:
– Subjects on inhaled tobramycin or other chronically inhaled antibiotics should remain on
that regimen throughout the trial.
– Subjects on cycle on and off inhaled antibiotics should continue on their prior schedule.
The timing of the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA in the Run-in Period should be
synchronized as closely as possible (e.g., not more than ±3 days).
– Subjects who alternate between two different inhaled antibiotics should remain on the
same cycling schedule during the trial. The timing of the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA in
the Run-in Period should be synchronized as closely as possible (e.g., not more than ±3
days) to the first day in the cycle onto one of the inhaled antibiotics.

•

Subjects may receive doses of prednisone or prednisolone of up to 10 mg/day chronically.
Subjects could not receive prednisone or prednisolone at doses of >60 mg daily for longer
than a 5-day period.

•

Caution and appropriate monitoring should be used when coadministration of trial drugs with
medicinal products that are substrates of OATP1B1/1B3, such as statins, glyburide,
nateglinide, and repaglinide.

Overall, the trial endpoints are acceptable and consistent with other similar phase 3 trials in this
population. They evaluate a number of factors considered to be clinically meaningful to patients
with CF including ppFEV1, CFQ-R RD, BMI, SwCl, weight, and CF PExs, although these
endpoints were outside of the statistical hierarchy. Further discussions regarding the use of
SwCl-based key secondary endpoints are included in Section 6.3.3.
Efficacy Assessments
Spirometry
Spirometry was performed according to the American Thoracic Society/European Respiratory
Society guidelines (Graham et al. 2019). Pre-bronchodilator was defined as spirometry
performed for subjects who withheld short-acting bronchodilators for ≥4 hours, long-acting
bronchodilator for ≥12 hours, and once-daily long-acting bronchodilator ≥24 hours prior to the
spirometry assessment.
During the Screening period, spirometry assessments were able to be performed pre- or postbronchodilator. At all other visits, spirometry assessments were performed pre-bronchodilator
and, during the treatment period, before trial drug dosing at approximately the same time each
visit.
Measured spirometry values were converted to percent predicted values using standard equations
of the Global Lung Institute (Quanjer et al. 2012). Note these trials were conducted prior to the
recent American Thoracic Society statement on the use race-neutral approaches to pulmonary
function test (PFT) interpretation (Bhakta et al. 2023).
All spirometry data was transmitted to a centralized spirometry service for quality review. The
investigator’s assessments of spirometry results were used for the screening assessment and
determination of eligibility.
Sweat Chloride
SwCl is a pharmacodynamic measure of CFTR function. A SwCl concentration of ≥60mmol/L is
diagnostic of CF and ≤30mmol/L is the normal range (Farrell et al. 2017). The trials completed
the SwCl test (quantitative pilocarpine iontophoresis) using an approved collection device. The
341
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
protocol required collection before trial drug dosing. At each time point, two samples were
collected (one from each arm). Samples were sent to a central laboratory for testing and
interpretation.
CF Pulmonary Exacerbation Definition
A CF pulmonary exacerbation was defined as a new or change in antibiotic therapy (IV, inhaled,
or oral) for four of more of the following signs or symptoms:
•

Change in sputum

•

New or increased hemoptysis

•

Increased cough

•

Increased dyspnea

•

Malaise, fatigue, or lethargy

•

Temperature above 38°C (approximately 100.4°F)

•

Anorexia or weight loss

•

Sinus pain or tenderness

•

Change in sinus discharge

•

Change in physical examination of the chest

•

Decrease in pulmonary function by 10%

•

Radiographic change indicative of pulmonary infection.

Overall, the definition of CF pulmonary exacerbations used is acceptable and consistent with
similar pivotal trials in this population. It was recommended that the trial drug not be interrupted
during an exacerbation unless it was determined to be in the best interest of the subject, as
determined by the investigator.
Cystic Fibrosis Questionnaire-Revised
The CFQ-R is a disease-specific health-related quality of life measure for CF that consists of
generic and CF-specific scales grouped into three modules and nine domains. The scales measure
quality of life, health perception, and symptoms over a 2-week recall period. It is available in
age-appropriate versions: child age 6 through 11 years (interview format), child age 12-13 years
(self-reported format), adolescent/adult ages ≥14 years (self-reported), and a parent/caregiver
proxy format. The RD of the CFQ-R has also been utilized independently to evaluate for
respiratory symptoms relevant to patients with CF. In patients with stable respiratory symptoms
and chronic Pseudomonas aeruginosa infection, a difference of at least four points in the CFQ-R
RD score is generally considered the meaningful within patient change (Quittner et al. 2009).
Subjects were asked to complete the CFQ-R in their native language if validated translations
were available. If no validated translation was available in the subject’s native language, the
subject did not complete the questionnaire. Subjects 12 and 13 years of age at the date of

Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
informed consent were asked to complete the CFQ-R Child version themselves, and their
parents/caregivers completed the CFQ-R Parent version, at all visits, regardless of whether the
subject subsequently turned 14 years of age during the trial. Subjects 14 years of age and older at
the date of informed consent completed the Adolescent/Adult version of the questionnaire
themselves at all visits.
Height and Weight
Weight and height were measured with shoes off. Following screening, height was collected only
for subjects ≤21 years of age on the date of informed consent.
Ophthalmologic Examination
Ophthalmologic examinations were only obtained in subjects <18 years of age on the date of
informed consent. All examinations were conducted by a licensed ophthalmologic or optometrist
and included the measurement of best-corrected distance visual acuity of each eye and
pharmacologically-dilated examination of the lens with a slit lamp. A screening examination was
not required if there was documentation of an examination meeting the protocol requirements
within the 3 months before the date of informed consent. No examinations were required in
subjects with documentation of bilateral lens removal.
Examinations occurred at screening and within 4 weeks prior to completion of trial participation
(in subjects who have completed at least 12 weeks of trial drug treatment).
Safety
Safety evaluations will include AEs, clinical laboratory assessments, clinical evaluation of vital
signs, ECGs, PExs, and pulse oximetry.
Statistical Analysis Plan
Sample Size and Power
The primary null hypothesis to be tested was that the mean absolute change in ppFEV1 from
baseline through Week 24 for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is inferior by >3 percentage points compared to
ELX/TEZ/IVA.
The noninferiority (NI) margin represented a clinically acceptable loss of effectiveness as
outlined in Non-Inferiority Clinical Trials to Establish Effectiveness (November 2016) regulatory
guidance. Furthermore, a statistical approach using the Rothmann method recommends that the
NI margin preserves at least 50% of the treatment effect of the active control (ELX/TEZ/IVA)
compared to placebo (Schumi and Wittes 2011), where the treatment effect is estimated by the
lower bound of the 95% CI. In the overall population eligible for Trials 102 and 103, the lower
bound of the 95% CI of the treatment effect for ELX/TEZ/IVA was approximately 12 percentage
points for ppFEV1. The selected noninferiority margin of three percentage points was consistent
with this statistical method for ppFEV1 in clinical studies evaluating CF treatments.
For Trial 102, assuming a within-group standard deviation (SD) of 8 and a 10% drop-out rate at
Week 24 and a treatment difference of 0 between VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA, a
343

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
sample size of 200 subjects in each group for a total of 400 subjects would have more than 90%
power to test the primary hypothesis for the primary endpoint, based on a 1-sided, 2-sample t-test
at a significance level of 0.025.
For Trial 103, assuming a within-group standard deviation (SD) of 8 and a 10% drop-out rate at
Week 24 and a treatment difference of 0 between VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA, a
sample size of 275 subjects in each group for a total of 550 subjects would have more than 95%
power to test the primary hypothesis for the primary endpoint, based on a 1-sided, 2-sample t-test
at a significance level of 0.025.
Noninferiority Margin for Trial 103
Although placebo-controlled data with ELX/TEZ/IVA in direct comparison with placebo was
not available for F/F, F/G, and F/RF subjects, a treatment effect could be estimated using
Bucher’s Indirect Comparison method by pooling data from Studies 445-103/105 and 661-106
for F/F subjects, Studies 445-104 and 770-102 for F/G subjects, and Studies 445-104 and 661108 for F/RF subjects (see Table 181).

Primary Estimand
The primary estimand is defined as the following:
•

Treatment: VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA versus ELX/TEZ/IVA

•

Population: Trial population represented by inclusion and exclusion criteria

•

Variable: Absolute change from baseline (after ELX/TEZ/IVA run-in) in ppFEV1 through
Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24)

•

Handling of intercurrent events:
– The treatment policy strategy will be used to handle the use of non-trial drug that affects
CFTR processing or function for >3 days in either the Run-in Period or the Treatment
Period prior to Week 24, which means that observed ppFEV1 values will be used, if
available, after this prohibited medication use.
– The treatment policy strategy will be used to handle treatment discontinuation prior to
Week 24, which means that observed ppFEV1 values will be used, if available, after
treatment discontinuation.

•

Population level summary: Difference in variable means between VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and
ELX/TEZ/IVA groups

Primary Analysis
The primary null hypothesis tested was that the mean for the primary efficacy variable for
VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA was inferior by >3 percentage points compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA. The
primary analysis of the primary estimand was performed using a mixed-effects model for
repeated measures (MMRM) with change from baseline at Day 15, Week 4, Week 8, Week 16,
and Week 24 as the dependent variable. For Trial 102, the model included fixed categorical
effects for treatment, visit, age at screening (<18 versus ≥18 years of age), and treatment-by-visit
interaction, with baseline ppFEV1 and baseline SwCl as continuous covariates. The model
estimated using restricted maximum likelihood. Denominator degrees of freedom for the F-test
for fixed effects were estimated using the Kenward-Roger approximation. An unstructured
covariance structure was used to model the within-subject errors. If the model estimation did not
converge, an alternative covariance structure like compound symmetry would have been used.
For Trial 103, the model included an additional fixed categorical effect for genotype group (F/F,
F/G, F/RF, TCR/non-F). If the model estimation did not converge, genotype group was
considered with two levels (F/F and non-F/F) or removed from the model, and if nonconvergence persisted, an alternative covariance structure like compound symmetry would have
used.
Conditional on the observed data and covariates, missing data was assumed to be missing at
random.
The primary result obtained from the model was the estimated treatment difference through
Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24). The adjusted mean with a 2-sided 95% CI
and the 1-sided P value for noninferiority was provided. The primary null hypothesis was
rejected, and noninferiority demonstrated, if the lower bound of the CI is ≥­3.0.
346
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
The primary null hypothesis of noninferiority described above was tested based on the full
analysis set (FAS).
If noninferiority for the primary endpoint was demonstrated, and if the lower bound of the same
95% CI was greater than 0, then superiority for the primary endpoint was also demonstrated, and
the 2-sided P value for superiority would be presented.
Supplementary Analysis
An alternative estimand was defined similarly to the primary estimand with the exception that
intercurrent events were addressed using the hypothetical strategy. In the hypothetical strategy,
observed values after intercurrent events were set to missing to provide a conservative and robust
test of noninferiority for the primary endpoint. Supplementary analysis of this alternative
estimand was performed similarly to the primary analysis.
Subgroup Analysis
Subgroup analyses of the primary efficacy endpoint were performed for each of the following
subgroups:
•

Age at screening (<18; ≥18 years)

•

ppFEV1 at baseline (<70; ≥70 percentage points)

•

SwCl at baseline (<30; ≥30mmol/L)

•

Sex (male; female)

•

Geographic region (North America [including United States and Canada]; Rest of the World
[including Europe, Israel, Australia, and New Zealand])

The MMRM used for the primary analysis of the primary estimand was used for the subgroup
analyses, where the similar model was applied to each category of the subgroup.
Analysis of Key Secondary Efficacy Variables
Absolute Change From Baseline in SwCl Through Week 24
The analysis of absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24 was based on an
MMRM similar to the primary analysis of the primary estimand, with absolute change from
baseline in SwCl at Day 15, Week 4, Week 16, and Week 24 as the dependent variable. The
estimated treatment difference through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24) was
presented along with the 2-sided 95% CI and P value.
Proportion of Subjects With SwCl <60mmol/L Through Week 24 (Pooled With Data From
Trials 102 and 103)
The response corresponding to SwCl <60mmol/L at each visit through Week 24 in the pooled
FAS was analyzed using a generalized estimating equations model. The model included fixed
categorical effects for treatment, age at screening (<18 versus ≥18 years of age), genotype group
(F/MF, F/F, F/G, F/RF, TCR/non-F), visit, and treatment-by-visit interaction, with baseline
347
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
ppFEV1 and baseline SwCl as continuous covariates. A logit link function and an unstructured
working correlation matrix were used in the generalized estimating equations model. If the
model estimation did not converge, genotype group was considered with three levels (F/MF, F/F,
and Other) or removed from the model, and if nonconvergence persists, an alternative covariance
structure like compound symmetry would have been used. The estimated odds ratio through
Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24) along with the 2-sided 95% CI and P value
was presented. The estimates for each visit through Week 24 were also presented.
Proportion of Subjects With SwCl <30mmol/L Through Week 24 (Pooled With Data From
Trials 102 and 103)
The analysis was similar to that for the pooled analysis of proportion of subjects with SwCl
<60mmol/L through Week 24.
Multiplicity Adjustment
A hierarchical testing procedure was used to control the overall type I error at an alpha of 0.05.
The key secondary endpoints were formally tested at an alpha level of 0.05 only if the primary
analysis of absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 24 was statistically
significant, i.e., if the null hypothesis of inferiority had been rejected. For a test at any step to be
considered statistically significant within the testing hierarchy, it must be statistically significant,
and all previous tests (if any) within the hierarchy must be statistically significant at the 0.05
level (one-sided 0.025 level for the primary endpoint). The testing order of the key secondary
endpoints was as follows:
•

Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24

•

Proportion of subjects with SwCl <60mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from
Trials 102 and 103)

•

Proportion of subjects with SwCl <30mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from
Trials 102 and 103)

Analysis of Other Secondary Efficacy Variables
Pulmonary Exacerbations
The number of PEx through Week 52 was analyzed using a negative binomial model with term
for treatment, and the estimated PEx rate (per year, with 48 weeks as 1 year) was presented. The
difference in PEx rate and the associated 95% CI was provided.
Absolute Change From Baseline in CFQ-R RD Score Through Week 24
The analysis of absolute change from baseline in CFQ-R RD score through Week 24 was based
on an MMRM similar to the primary analysis of the primary estimand, with absolute change
from baseline in CFQ-R RD score at Week 8, Week 16, and Week 24 as the dependent variable.
The estimated treatment difference through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24)
was presented along with the 2-sided 95% CI.

Absolute Change From Baseline in ppFEV1 Through Week 52
Analysis of this endpoint was based on an MMRM similar to the primary analysis of the primary

Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 52
Analysis of this endpoint was based on an MMRM similar to that for absolute change from
baseline in SwCl through Week 24, with the addition of data from Week 36 and Week 52. The
estimated change through Week 52 was based on averaging estimates from Weeks 16, 24, 36 and
52.
Analysis Population Definitions
•

All subject set: all subjects who were randomized or received at least one dose of trial drug

•

FAS: all randomized subjects who carry the intended CFTR allele variant(s) and received at
least one dose of trial drug during the treatment period

•

Safety analysis set: Included all subjects who received at least one dose of trial drug. This
was broken down into the Safety Set for the Run-in Period (subjects who received at least
one dose of trial drug during the Run-in Period) and the Safety Set for the Treatment Period
(subjects who received at least one dose of trial drug during the Treatment Period).

•

Per-protocol set: a subset of the FAS that excluded subjects with certain important protocol
deviations and other situations

Adverse Events
The Applicant provided accurate definitions of AEs and SAEs consistent with 21 CFR 312.32.
Trial assessments including laboratory tests, ECGs, PExs, and vital signs were assessed and
those deemed to have clinically significant worsening from baseline were documented as an AE.
All AEs were collected from the time of informed consent until the subject completed the trial
participation. Treatment-emergent (TE) time periods were broken down in to TE period of the
run-in period (from the first dose date of trial drug in the run-in period to the first dose date of
the trial drug in the treatment period) and the TE period for the treatment period (from the first
dose of trial drug in the treatment period to 28 days after the last dose of the trial drug or to the
complete of trial participation, whichever occurs first).
AE severity was graded using the Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE),
Version 5.0, Cancer Therapy Evaluation Program toxicity scale or as mild, moderate, severe,
life-threating, or death. Causality was assessed as related, possibly related, unlikely related, and
not related. AEs were coded using the MedDRA dictionary version 26.1.
Overall, the Applicant’s AE and SAE definitions and use of CTCAE severity scale were
acceptable and consistent with other CF development programs.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Adverse Events of Special Interest
Liver function test abnormalities, creatinine kinase elevations, rash, cataracts, hypoglycemic
events, and neuropsychiatric events were designated as adverse events of special interest
(AESIs). These were considered appropriate given the known safety profile of medications
similar to VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA (e.g., ELX/TEZ/IVA) and a review of the safety information from
the phase 2 development program.
Protocol Amendments
Trial 102, initially dated April 27, 2021, was amended two times. The key changes in each
amendment are noted below:
•

Version 2.0 (July 12, 2021) – Clarified eligible MF definition.

•

Version 3.0 (August 19, 2021) – Increased sample size and treatment duration (with updates
to trial endpoints to reflect this), added designation of AESIs (reviewed above), added that
the analysis of the primary and key secondary endpoints through Week 24 will be estimated
by averaging Week 16 and 14, and provided rationale for the noninferiority margin for the
primary analysis.

Trial 103, also initially dated April 27, 2021, was amended two times. The key changes in each
amendment are noted below:
•

Version 2.0 (July 12, 2021) – Minor changes.

•

Version 3.0 (August 19, 2021) – Expanded trial population to include subjects who have at
least one CFTR variant identified as ELX/TEZ/IVA responsive based on in vitro data and no
F508del variant, increased sample size and treatment duration (with updates to trial
endpoints to reflect this), added designation of AESIs (reviewed above), included that
stratification by genotype was to be based on F/F, F/G, F/RF, and TCR/non-F genotypes,
added that the analysis of the primary and key secondary endpoints through Week 24 will be
estimated by averaging Week 16 and 14, and provided rationale for the noninferiority margin
for the primary analysis.

COVID-19 Impact
The trial protocols include safety measures to provide subjects the opportunity to continue to
participate in the trial while ensuring safety and minimizing risk of coronavirus disease of 2019
(COVID-19). The Applicant reports that in general, there the impact of COVID-19 on the trial
conduct was minimal.
Protocol Deviations
An important protocol deviation was defined as any protocol deviation that may have significant
impact the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of key trial data or that may have
significantly impacted a subject’s rights, safety, or well-being.
In Trial 102, the Applicant reported that 20 (5.0%) of subjects had 21 important protocol
deviations (VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA: 11, 5.6%; ELX/TEZ/IVA: 9, 4.5%). The deviation summaries
350
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
provided were reviewed and included subject use of prohibited concomitant medications (e.g.,
commercially available medications that affect CFTR processing or function while an SAE was
investigated) and enrollment of subjects who did not meet eligibility criteria (e.g., use of
antibiotics or acute respiratory infection during the run-in period).
In Trial 103, the Applicant reported that 27 (4.7%) of subjects had important protocol deviations
(VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA: 15, 5.3%; ELX/TEZ/IVA: 12, 4.2%). The deviation summaries provided
were reviewed and included enrollment of subjects who did not meet eligibility criteria (e.g., use
of antibiotics or acute respiratory infection during the run-in period) and use of prohibited
concomitant medications (e.g., commercially available or compassionate-use medications that
affect CFTR drug processing or function) when protocol-defined criteria were met (e.g., AST
levels >8x ULN). The number of subjects with protocol deviations related specifically to the
investigational product (e.g., trial drug dispensation errors, compliance, trial drug kits not being
returned, and incorrect trial drug dosing) occurred at higher rates in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
treatment arm (11, 3.9%) than the ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment arm (5, 1.7%).
Overall, the rates protocol deviations were small and consistent with other similarly sized trials.
The reported protocol deviations are unlikely to affect the overall analysis of the phase 3 pivotal
trials.

15.2. Pediatric Cohort (6 Through 11 Years Old),
Trial 105
Overview and Objectives
Trial VX21-121-105 (Trial 105) was a phase 3, 2-part (Parts A and B), open-label, multicohort,
multicenter trial evaluating the PK, safety, tolerability, and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA triple
combination therapy in subjects with CF 1 through 11 years of age. The Applicant submitted the
interim analysis report of the final data from subjects 6 through 11 years of age (Cohorts A1 and
B1) concurrently with the pivotal phase 3 trials in adults in adolescents.
Trial 105 Title: A Phase 3 Study Evaluating the Pharmacokinetics, Safety, and Tolerability of
VX-121 [VNZ] /Tezacaftor/Deutivacaftor Triple Combination Therapy [VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA] in
Cystic Fibrosis Subjects 1 Through 11 Years of Age.
•

Cohort 1 Trial dates: June 21, 2022 to November 27, 2023

•

Cohort 1 Trial report date: April 3, 2024

The design of Cohort 1 and the overall pediatric multicohort trial is consistent with other trials
performed to support pediatric indications in this population. The consideration of efficacy
extrapolation for the 6- to 11-year-old population from the adult and adolescent population is
appropriate given the similarly in the disease process between the populations.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Part A Primary Objectives
•

To evaluate the PK of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA and relative metabolites when dosed in triple
combination

•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA

Part B Primary Objective
•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA through Week 24

Part B Secondary Objectives
•

To evaluate the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA through Week 24

•

To evaluate the PK or VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites

Trial Design
Trial 105 was a phase 3, 2-part (Parts A and B), multicenter, multicohort, open-label trial in CF
subjects 1 through 11 years of age (inclusive). The cohorts included in Trial 105 were based on
subject age:
•

Cohorts A1 and B1: Subjects 6 through 11 years of age

•

Cohorts A2 and B2: Subjects 2 through 5 years of age

•

Cohorts A3 and B3: Subjects 1 to less than 2 years of age

This review will include and refer to Cohorts A1 and B1 only unless otherwise noted. The
remainder of the cohorts in Trial 105 are ongoing or have not yet started enrollment at the time
of the review.
In Part A, the PK portion, there was a 28-day screening period followed by a 15-day treatment
period. PK samples were collected on Days 1, 8, and 15 of the treatment period. A final safety
follow-up visit occurred 28 days after the last dose of the trial drug. Trial subjects were eligible
to be screened for enrollment in Part B. A review of safety, tolerability, and available PK data
was completed by the Applicant’s internal team after Cohort A1 completed treatment to
determine the doses and weight cutoffs for Cohort B1.
Part B, the primary safety portion, also included a 28-day screening period. Subjects who were
not already receiving stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment entered the 4-week (28-day) run-in period
and received the approved ELX/TEZ/IVA dose. Stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment was defined as
receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA for at least 28 days prior to the screening visit. This run-in period was
waved for subjects already receiving stable ELX/TEZ/IVA. During the treatment period, all
subjects received VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA for 24 weeks. Eligible subjects were able to enroll in the
optional open-label extension trial (Trial VX22-121-106). A safety follow-up visit occurred at 28
(±7) days after the last dose of the trial drug but was not required for those who completed the
treatment period and transitioned within 28 days of the last dose of trial drug to one of the
following: a commercially available Applicant medication that affects CFTR processing or

Trial Population
Trial 105 enrolled pediatric subjects (ages 6 through 11 years) with stable CF per the enrollment
criteria outlined below:
Key Inclusion Criteria
•

Ages 6 through 11 years (inclusive), on the date of informed consent
– Subjects who completed Cohort A1 but were ≥12 years of age on the date of informed
consent in Cohort B1 are not eligible to enroll in Cohort B1.

•

Weight between 5th and 9th percentile (inclusive) for weight-for-age at the Screening Visit
based on current CDC growth charts

•

Confirmed diagnosis of CF as determined by the investigator

•

At least one triple combination responsive variant (including F508del) in the CFTR gene

•

FEV1≥60% predicted at screening (Quanjer et al. 2012)

•

Stable CF disease as judged by the investigator

•

Negative serum pregnancy test at screening for female subjects of childbearing potential

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
Key Exclusion Criteria
•

History of comorbidity that might confound the results in the opinion of the investigator.
Including, but not limited to:
– Hepatic cirrhosis with portal hypertension, moderate hepatic impairment (Child Pugh
Score 7 to 9), severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh Score 10 to 15)
– Chronic kidney disease (Stage 3 or above)
– Sold organ or hematological transplantation
– Alcohol or drug abuse in the past year
– Cancer

•

Any clinically significant laboratory abnormalities at screening that would interfere with the
trial assessments or pose an under risk for the subject

•

History of intolerance to trial drug that would pose additional risk to the subject in the
opinion of the investigator (e.g., subjects with a history of LFT elevations requiring treatment
interruption or discontinuation, allergy, or hypersensitivity to the trial drug)

•

Any of the following abnormal labs at screening:
– Hemoglobin <10 g/dL
– Total bilirubin ≥2 x ULN
– AST, ALT, gamma-glutamyl transferase, or alkaline phosphatase (ALP) ≥3 x ULN
– Abnormal renal function defined as glomerular filtration rate ≤45 mL/min/1.73 m2
(Counahan et al. 1976)

•

An acute upper or lower respiratory tract infection, infectious PEx of CF, or changes in
therapy (including antibiotics) for sinopulmonary disease within 28 days before Day 1 (first
dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA)

•

Lung infection with organisms associated with a more rapid decline in pulmonary status
(including but not limited to Burkholderia cenocepacia, Burkholderia dolosa, and
Mycobacterium abscessus). For subjects who have had a history of a positive culture, the
investigator will apply the following criteria to establish whether the subject is free of
infection with such organisms:
– The subject has not had a respiratory tract culture positive for these organisms within the
12 months before the date of informed consent/assent
– The subject has at least two respiratory tract cultures negative for these organisms within
the 12 months before the day of informed consent, with the first and last of these
separated by at least 3 months, and the most recent one within the 6 months before the
date of informed consent/assent

Ongoing or prior participation in a trial of an investigational treatment other than Vertex

Protocol Deviations
An important protocol deviation was defined as any protocol deviation that may have
significantly impacted the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of key trial data or that may
have significantly impacted a subject’s rights, safety, or well-being.
In Part A, two subjects were reported as having protocol deviations due to enrollment without
documented review of screening laboratory analyses and no baseline hemoglobin value
confirmed prior to screening. In Part B, an additional two subjects were reported as having
important protocol deviations both related to incorrect dispension of trial drug (both subjects
received the incorrect (≥40 kg) kit at Day 1 but where dispensed the correct (<40 kg) kit on Day
15). The rates of protocol deviations were small and unlikely to affect the overall analysis of
Trial 105.

ppFEV1 Through Week 52
The Applicant evaluated the absolute change from baseline of the ppFEV1 through Week 52,
which the Applicant defined as the average of the estimated treatment effects in Weeks 16, 24,
366
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
36, and 52. These endpoints supported the primary endpoint. As shown in Table 194, the mean
(95% CI) change from baseline in ppFEV1 through 52 weeks was 0.1% (-0.8%, 1.0%) in Trial
102 and 0.3 (-0.4%, 1.0%) in Trial 103.
SwCl Through Week 52
The Applicant also evaluated the absolute change from baseline of the SwCl through Week 52,
which the Applicant defined as the average of the estimated treatment effects in Weeks 16, 24,
36, and 52. VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment led to lower SwCl through 52 weeks compared to
ELX/TEZ/IVA. The mean (95% CI) absolute change in SwCl through 52 weeks was -8.0 (-9.9, 6.1) mmol/L and -2.8 (-4.6, -1.0) mmol/L in Trials 102 and 103, respectively. The clinical
significance of this unclear and reviewed in more detail in Section 6.3.2.
Responder Analysis: Proportion of Subjects With SwCl <60mmol/L Through Week 24
The Applicant presented the odds ratio for the proportion of subjects with a SwCl <60mmol/L
for the pooled phase 3 population and by individual trial. Subjects treated with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
were more likely to have a SwCl <60mmol/L through Week 24 than subjects treated with
ELX/TEZ/IVA in the pooled phase 3 population: odds ratio (95% CI): 2.21 (1.55, 3.15),
p<0.0001. Similar results were demonstrated when the phase 3 trials were analyzed separately,
although in Trial 103, the 95% confidence interval did not exclude the null. These results are
supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, but the overall clinical significance is unclear, as
reviewed in more detail in Section 6.3.2.
Responder Analysis: Proportion of Subjects With SwCl <30mmol/L Through Week 24
The Applicant also presented the odds ratio for the proportion of subjects with a SwCl
<30mmol/L. Similar to the responder analysis of subjects below the CF diagnostic threshold,
subjects treated with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA were more likely to have a SwCl <30mmol/L through
Week 24 than subjects treated with ELX/TEZ/IVA in the pooled phase 3 population: odds ratio
(95% CI): 2.87 (2.00, 4.12), p<0.0001. Similar results were demonstrated when the phase 3 trials
were analyzed separately, and for this analysis, the 95% confidence intervals excluded the null in
both trials. These results are supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, but the overall
clinical significance is unclear, as reviewed in more detail in Section 6.3.2.

16.2.2. Pediatric Cohort (6 Through 11 Years Old),
Trial 105
Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
As noted previously, efficacy in the 6- to 11-year-old CF population was extrapolated from
adults and adolescents. The Applicant also evaluated a number of exploratory efficacy endpoints
in the 6- to 11-year-old population as reviewed in Table 195. The lack of a control arm limits the
ability to interpret these data, but, in general, the results support the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/DIVA in this age range.

Abbreviations: BMI, body mass index; CFB, change from baseline; CFQ-R RD, cystic fibrosis questionnaire-revised respiratory

Drug Acceptability Assessment at Week 24
The drug acceptability assessment was evaluated using the Modified Hedonic Scale at Week 24.
Only 2 out of 74 subjects (2.8%) indicated that they disliked the medication (“disliked it a little”
or “disliked it very much”) and 24 (32.4%) subjects responded that they were neutral. The
remainder of subjects 48 (64.8%) indicated they liked the medication (“liked it a little” or
“disliked it very much”). These results are supportive of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in this population.
Number of PEx and CF-Related Hospitalizations Through Week 24
The Applicant recorded the number of PEx including PEx requiring hospitalization or IV
antibiotics therapy. Overall, six subjects (7.7%) experienced PExs with one subject (1.3%)
hospitalized and requiring IV antibiotic therapy. The lack of comparator arm limits the analysis
of these data, but these rates appear to be consistent with published data in CF patients in this age
range.
CFQ-R RD Through Week 24
Treatment with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA lead to an increase in the mean CFQ-R RD score through 24
weeks of 3.9 points. This is supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. The magnitude of
the absolute mean change from baseline is smaller than the changes observed in the
ELX/TEZ/IVA trial in this population but higher than the adult and adolescent population in the
phase 3 trials. The significance of this difference is unclear given the lack of control arm.
BMI, Weight, Height, and BMI Z-Scores at Week 24
Treatment with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA resulted in within subject increases in absolute change from
baseline for weight (1.67 kg), height (2.7 cm), and BMI (0.22 kg/m2) at Week 24. BMI z-scores
remained similar over this period (-0.05). The assessment of these changes is limited by the
absence of a comparator arm, and it is unclear if the observed absolute increases in weight and
height reflect normal development or a treatment effect. Based on CDC growth charts for healthy
368
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5500284

NDA 218730
ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
children, the average weight gain and growth for children between the age of 6 through 11 years
is approximately 1-3 kg and 4-6 cm (NCHS 2024). While the observed change in stature is
below the CDC growth chart averages in healthy children, the stability of the BMI z-score is
reassuring.
Proportion of Subjects With SwCl <60mmol/L or <30mmol/L Through Week 24
The mean SwCl at Week 24 was 40.4mmol/L. The proportion of subjects with a SwCl less than
60mmol/L was 94.9% at Week 24 and 52.6% of subjects had a SwCl less than 30mmol/L at
Week 24. The ability to interpret these results is limited by the lack of control arm. In addition,
the clinical meaningfulness of these endpoints is unclear, as discussed in further detail in
Section 6.3.2.
Other Efficacy Endpoints
The Applicant also evaluated multiple other efficacy endpoints including fecal elastase-1, serum
immunoreactive trypsinogen, fecal calprotectin, lung clearance index 2.5, and levels of the fatsoluble vitamins. The evaluation of these results is limited because of the lack of comparator arm
and because these are exploratory endpoints.

that were identified.

Overall, 395 (38.3%) of subjects reported an AE during the run-in period with 11 (1.1%) subjects
reporting an SAE. The most common AEs reported were headache, cough, and COVID-19. The
most common AE leading to drug discontinuation was infective pulmonary exacerbation of CF.
Overall, the review of the AEs in the run-in period did not reveal any new safety signals.
Adverse Events in the Follow-Up Period
The Applicant reported that 822 (84.6%) subjects from Trials 102 and 103 enrolled in the openlabel extension, Trial VX20-121-104 (Trial 104). Of those subjects who did not enroll in Trial
104 and entered the run-in period, the review of AEs and SAEs did not reveal any new safety
concerns. The most common AE reported during this time was infective pulmonary exacerbation
of CF.

17.3. Adverse Events, Trial 105 (Cohort A1)
AEs occurring in Cohort A1 of Trial 105 were also reviewed. Overall, 12 (70.6%) subjects
experienced an AE. All AEs were considered mild to moderate and 3 (17.6%) subjects
experienced AEs that were determined to be related by the investigator. There were no deaths
and no SAEs reported during Cohort A1. The most common AEs reported in subjects were
cough (3, 17.6%), abdominal pain (2, 11.8%), and rash (2, 11.8%). There were no new safety
signals identified during the review of the safety data from Cohort A1 of Trial 105.

17.4. Additional Safety Analyses
Electrocardiograms
No clinically significant ECG trends or safety concerns were identified in the phase 3 trials of
VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. We note there was a numerically higher proportion of subjects in the
VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment group with a decrease of ≥20 beats per minute from baseline
compared to the ELX/TEZ/IVA (8.6% versus 6.5%). This finding is unlikely to be clinically
significant. AEs related to ECG findings were similar between the treatment groups.
QT Study
VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA did not prolong the QTcF interval in the thorough QT study assessment
(Study VX22-121-013). This was reviewed by the Interdisciplinary Review team for Cardiac
Safety Studies (review dated July 16, 2024).
Safety Specific Clinical Trials
The Applicant submitted a 90-day safety update that consisted of updates that became available
since the safety data cut for the NDA submission and included any deaths, all new SAEs, AEs
leading to treatment discontinuation, and pregnancies from February 13, 2024, to May 15, 2024,
for ongoing studies in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA program.
The safety update included the following trials listed in Table 200:

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for
major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 202). The PI
was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent with
labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed
for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the
healthcare practitioner.

• The Warnings and Precautions were updated to include

• A warning was added regarding the potential risks and
uncertainty in patients who discontinued or interrupted ELX,

• A warning was added to include the risk of hypersensitivity
reactions, consistent with ELX/TEZ/IVA.
• The remaining warnings and precautions include the risk of
reduced effectiveness with concomitant use of CYP3A inducers,
the risk of ARs with concomitant use with CYP3A inhibitors, and
the risk of cataracts. The Warnings and Precautions are similar
to those of ELX/TEZ/IVA and medications that contain one or
more of its components.

• This section was updated to include potential reactions based
on the data from the phase 3 clinical trials. As these were
noninferiority trials without a placebo-controlled treatment arm,
ARs were defined as events occurring at or above a threshold of
5% and ≥1% higher frequency in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA exposed
patients compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA exposed patients.

available in patients who could not tolerate ELX/TEZ/IVA.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
The following postmarketing requirements and commitments are being issued at the time of
regulatory action.
PMR 4734-1
Conduct a 2-year carcinogenicity study in rats administered vanzacaftor.
Study Completion:

02/2025

Final Report Submission:

04/2025

PMR 4734-2
Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to assess the effect of multiple doses of VNZ/TEZ/DIVA on the single-dose pharmacokinetics of a substrate of BCRP, to address the potential for
increased drug toxicity and provide appropriate drug interaction management strategies for
coadministration of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA with this substrate. Design and conduct the study in
accordance with the FDA guidance for industry Clinical Drug Interaction Studies —Cytochrome
P450 Enzyme- and Transporter-Mediated Drug Interactions (January 2020).
Final Protocol Submission:

06/2025

Trial Completion:

05/2026

Final Report Submission:

11/2026

25. Financial Disclosure
Table 203 summarizes the financial disclosures submitted to the NDA that include Trial 102,
103, and 105. There were several significant payments that included speaker fees, steering
committee and advisory board fees, and investigator-initiated grants. No investigator reported
that the payments received were related to the clinical study.
The payments were unlikely to have a significant impact upon the conduct of the clinical trials,
given that the majority of individual sites enrolled a small number of subjects and the pivotal
efficacy trials were randomized, double-blinded, and controlled with objective spirometric,
nutritional, and exacerbation related endpoints. In addition, site-based sensitivity analyses were
performed on the primary endpoint and the removal of the subjects from any individual site does
not affect the evaluation of efficacy or safety evaluation of this NDA.
